# AETHERIS — Research → Implementation Report (2026-08-21)

**Scope.** Rigorous sweep of current research (weighted to 2026-03 … 2026-08) across ten angles, mapped to concrete AETHERIS code changes. Angles 1–7 carry full source inventories; Angles 8–10 are condensed to load-bearing findings (source scratch lost — see each angle's scope note). Ends with a single [ROI-ranked cross-angle backlog](#cross-angle-backlog-roi-ranked), the [gaps in this pass](#gaps-in-this-pass), and [corrections to prior AETHERIS research](#corrections-to-prior-aetheris-research).

**Nothing is deduplicated and nothing is dropped**, per the brief. Deliberate cross-angle overlaps are marked where they occur, and every angle keeps its full *Unverified* and *Rejected* appendices verbatim — an idea that failed verification is recorded as failed, not deleted.

**Verification rule used throughout.** Every cited source was fetched (arXiv `/abs/` page, ACL Anthology landing page, model card, vendor doc, PyPI page, repo). A source is only in a main table if (a) the page exists, (b) the title matches, (c) the quoted number appears on the page. Numbers that only appeared in search snippets were dropped or moved to the *Unverified* appendix. This project's earlier research contained a fabricated CVE; that is the failure mode this protocol exists to prevent.

**Follow-up audit, 2026-08-21.** The non-arXiv sources behind Angles 1 and 8 were independently re-audited after the fact: **29 pages attempted, 25 reachable, 0 wrong identifiers, 0 dead pages, 0 licence mismatches**, one quoted number traced to the wrong page (FactCG's scores are on the LLM-AggreFact leaderboard, not its model card — now cited correctly), and one mis-quotation corrected (Gemini tuning). Four pages were unreachable behind auth walls. Corrections are applied inline where they belong, marked in place rather than listed separately.

**Second follow-up audit, 2026-08-21 — every arXiv ID in Angles 1 and 2.** A regex sweep found **143 distinct arXiv IDs** between `## Angle 1` and `## Angle 3`; all 143 were re-fetched from the arXiv Atom API (`https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?id_list=…`, batched 15 per call, plus the 4 Angle-7 IDs already flagged for date anomalies = **147 checked**). Result: **0 fabricated IDs, 0 that fail to resolve, 0 title-to-paper mismatches, 0 unreachable.** Every number quoted in an Angle 1 or Angle 2 main-table row or ranked idea was matched verbatim against the abstract, with three exceptions now marked in place (a page-count that lives in the arXiv comment field, the `2602.08159` "on GPT-2" qualifier, and MAST's unsupported NeurIPS venue tag). Author attributions: 30 checked, 30 correct. Venue tags: 8 confirmed from the comment field, 1 unsupported (MAST). **15 IDs carry a `published` timestamp outside the month their YYMM prefix implies** — 7 are ordinary 1–2-day month-boundary cases and are deliberately not flagged, 8 are genuine anomalies (4 were already recorded, 4 are newly marked inline: `2606.07528`, `2607.18241`, `2603.04421`, `2405.01563`). The highest-stakes rows — citation-fabrication papers `2605.08583` (CiteTracer) and `2607.18360` (HALLMARK), and the cascade pair `2606.07937` / `2606.07941` — are clean, numbers included. Raw JSON for both batches is on disk at `.research_tmp/out_main.json` and `.research_tmp/out_appendix.json`. Eight rows use a system name or a shortened title in the Source column where the paper's own title is longer; the ID-to-paper binding is correct in every case, and the one whose dropped clause changed the meaning (`2602.04853`) has been expanded.

**Relation to earlier docs.** [`AETHERIS_DEEP_RESEARCH.md`](AETHERIS_DEEP_RESEARCH.md) = architecture analysis. [`ISSUES_AND_FIXES.md`](ISSUES_AND_FIXES.md) = internal refactor debt. [`REFACTOR_FINDINGS.md`](REFACTOR_FINDINGS.md) = what landed. **This doc = external research → what to build.**

Legend: **E** = effort (S/M/L), **R** = risk, **ROI** = payoff. Links in the AETHERIS column are the files to touch. Angles 7–10 omit E/R/ROI deliberately: their source passes gave no effort or risk estimates, and inventing them would manufacture data.

---

## Angle 1 — Hallucination detection, grounding & abstention

Code baseline verified by the researching agent by reading `orchestrator/claims.py`, `validation_layer.py`, `uncertainty.py`, `consensus.py`, call sites in `pipelines.py`, and `requirements.txt` (**no `torch`/`transformers` today** — this constrains every "add a local encoder" option).

Adopt three things verbatim: a 0–1 support score into `metrics.py`; `groundingCheckRequired` (stop firewalling filler — `_sentence_looks_like_claim` fires on any `" is "`); sub-500ms as the inline latency bar | S | M (cloud dep) | **H** |

Make it CONTRADICTED, name the digit, exempt derived arithmetic | S | L | **H** |

### Implementation ideas, ranked

1. **One verifier interface, hosted default.** New `orchestrator/verifiers.py`: `async def score_support(claim, evidence) -> float`, called from `ClaimManager.validate_claim` (`claims.py:226`), deleting the `_keyword_set` ∩ `_overlap_threshold` decision (`claims.py:531,548`). Two backends: **hosted** (Bedrock `ApplyGuardrail`, or Vertex `checkGrounding` — one HTTPS call, no `torch`, and Vertex documents <500ms as achievable per inference) and **local** (`lettucedetect` 0.2.3, MIT; or HHEM-2.1-Open, Apache-2.0, <600MB RAM, ~1.5s/2k tokens CPU). Justification for spending anything at all: MiniCheck hits GPT-4 accuracy at 770M for 400× less, and NLI verification ranks first at **0.88 AUROC** vs 0.66 runner-up (HalluScan). Avoid Bespoke-MiniCheck-7B and Lynx — both CC BY-NC 4.0.
2. **Stop sibling agents grounding each other; weight evidence by type.** `build_evidence` (`claims.py:352`) pools `agent_outputs` and `prior_results` with `user_query` and history, and `EvidenceRecord` has no weight — so Creative's unsupported assertion can VERIFY Logician's identical assertion at confidence 0.85. That is the *recursive* edge measured at R=1.08 / amplification 1.34 undefended. Add `weight: float` from `_infer_evidence_type` (already computed at `claims.py:556`, then discarded) and require FACTUAL claims to have ≥1 non-`reasoning` record. GSAR's `complementary` category is worth adopting too — Creative produces exactly that, and the current binary calls it unsupported.
3. **Tiered recovery instead of a text qualifier.** `_qualify_unsupported_claim` (`claims.py:567`) appends `[Qualifier: …]` and ships. With FPR deciding deployability (HALLMARK) and a 1–3 shared-token threshold, that marker gets trained out of users' attention. Adopt GSAR **proceed/regenerate/replan** wired to `run_repair_loop` in `orchestrator/repair.py`, plus Azure-style **correction** (`correctedText`) for entity/number/version cases.
4. **Per-stage checks, not near-terminal.** Firewall runs at `pipelines.py:683` / `validation_layer.py:159`. CHARM: **82.1%** propagation reduction per-stage vs **18.5%** output-level, at **215ms ± 18ms** per stage. `pipelines.py:301` already builds evidence mid-pipeline. Instrument both directions — sequential refinement also cost **0.789 → 0.769** factual accuracy.
5. **Calibrated abstention replacing hardcoded confidences.** `claims.py:287,290` hardcode 0.85/0.25/0.2; `uncertainty.py:148` decides clarification from hand-written hints. Conformal abstention is post-hoc with finite-sample guarantees (**75% conditional correctness**) and needs only a calibration set. So log `(grounded_fraction, judge_score, judge_confidence, abstained, was_correct)` per request, then calibrate one threshold. Two reinforcing facts: reasoning fine-tuning **degrades abstention 24%** (AbstentionBench) so it can't be delegated to the agent model; and scoring that rewards guessing is the root cause (Kalai et al.) — which is what a 0–10 judge with no abstention credit does.
6. **Cheap-first cascade: logprobs → encoder → judge.** HALT needs only top-20 logprobs at 30× smaller / 60× faster than a ModernBERT-base encoder; LSC needs one forward pass; HalluScan's routing gets **2.0× cost cut for 0.1% AUROC**. Providers behind `api_gateway/client.py` already return logprobs. This also replaces `_sentence_looks_like_claim` (`claims.py:576`), which selects claims by substring-matching `" is "` and 14 other markers — it would firewall "This is a summary of your options."
7. **Code/math evidence needs a code-capable detector.** LettuceDetect-large scores **0.17** span-F1 on code-agent evidence where the v2 model gets **0.60** and the best zero-shot judges reach 0.22. Route by the evidence type already computed. Lynx's 12.8-pt FinanceBench deficit is the same warning for numbers/tables.
8. **Entity-attribution requirement.** `validate_claim` already refuses records whose numbers don't cover the claim's (`claims.py:254`) — a digit-only degenerate entity check. Deceptive Grounding measures **7.8%** DG in a production audit (13.6% for recent entities) and **97.0%/98.7%** for entity-attribution verification. Bag-of-keywords cannot catch it: `_keyword_set` discards word order.
9. **Fix evidence finding before the classifier.** PROBE finds evidence finding is the weakest of its four stages across all models. AETHERIS's is a dict-walk with no retrieval, while `retrieval.py` and `knowledge_layer.py` sit unwired. Highest leverage per line in this list, and it needs no new model.
10. **Keep context when decomposing.** `_split_sentences` (`claims.py:508`) splits on `[.!?]\s+` — the exact atomicity/context tradeoff TriQua names, and a driver of over-qualification. Add `context: str` to `Claim`; adopt `groundingCheckRequired` to skip filler.
11. **Perturbation grounding, gated to high-stakes claims.** GASP: **≈0.73**/**≈0.67** AUC with a 0.5–1.7B scorer, training-free threshold. Cost is 2+k passes for k chunks, and it **fails when the answer is available from parametric memory** — which describes much of AETHERIS's traffic, since retrieval often doesn't fire. Gate on stakes and on whether retrieval actually ran. CORTEX's contiguity smoothing is the free part.
12. **Local observer probe** — the only internal-state route behind closed APIs: **5–27 points** over baselines in one forward pass; feasibility is settled (**0.904–1.000 AUROC** from one mid-layer at 4-bit on 8GB, plus attention entropy **0.866–0.941** free from the same pass). Rules: 25 labels to start, but train jointly across task types or it transfers near-randomly.
13. **Deterministic-first citation stage** if any agent emits references: CiteTracer **97.1%** accuracy, class F1 97.0/95.8/98.5. Tune on FPR; expect over-flagging of post-cutoff papers.
14. **Build an AETHERIS-native eval set before trusting any of the above.** Four independent results make this a gate, not a nice-to-have: a plain LLM-judge is competitive with elaborate detectors (TRIVIA+); five factuality metrics are mutually inconsistent and biased against reworded + faraway-source outputs — i.e. against exactly what the Synthesis Judge and `build_evidence` produce (Verify with Caution); RAGTruth re-annotation finds **1.68×** more hallucinations than original labels; and a composite hallucination score correlated with experts at only **r=0.41**. Harvest a few hundred labeled examples from existing `ExecutionManifest` logs.

### Cheap wins (≤1 day each)

1. **Use the evidence type you already compute.** Add `weight: float = 1.0` to `EvidenceRecord`, set from `_infer_evidence_type` (`claims.py:556`), require ≥1 non-`reasoning` record for FACTUAL claims. ~10 lines = GSAR's typed weighting.
2. **Exclude sibling agent outputs from grounding FACTUAL claims.** One filter in `build_evidence`. Kills the circular-grounding edge.
3. **Promote number mismatch from silent skip to explicit verdict.** `claims.py:254` does `continue` on mismatch, so the claim lands UNVERIFIED with no reason. Make it CONTRADICTED, name the digit, exempt derived arithmetic. A pure regex number gate measured **−66.2% / −86.0% / −64.8%** digit hallucinations for +5.3–9.0% latency.
4. **Emit a 0–1 support score per response** into `orchestrator/metrics.py` (Vertex semantics: fraction of claims grounded). Nothing in idea 5 is possible without this series, and it's the only way to know if a later change helped.
5. **Skip non-factual filler** — add `groundingCheckRequired` semantics to `_sentence_looks_like_claim` (`claims.py:576`). Straight FPR reduction.
6. **Add Force/Remove sanity tests** to `tests/orchestrator/test_claim_contradiction.py`. If verdicts don't move when a correct answer is swapped into an unchanged trace, the firewall reads endpoint cues. Nothing today would catch that.
7. **Contiguity-smooth per-sentence verdicts** — suppress isolated flags between two verified sentences. No model.
8. **Prefer low-hallucination models for the Judge role** in `strategy.py`/`routing.py` (gpt-5.4-nano 3.1% at 100% answer rate; gemini-2.5-flash-lite 3.3%) — reading the answer-rate column, not just the hallucination rate.

Vendor SEO content (giga.ai "zero latency cost", galileo.ai top-10 list with an unsourced Gartner figure, futureagi, dataaspirant, atlan, premai, zeroentropy, plus Medium/Substack RAGAS posts) — no methodology, and several contradict measured results above (CHARM's 215ms/stage, LettucePrevent's +5.3–9.1%). `github.com/firish/rag-rack` — personal blog, rejected as a citation, but its min-aggregation of HHEM + MiniCheck-Flan-T5-Large is a sound idea worth testing against your own eval set. **No verifiable academic critique of RAGAS by name was found** — treat "RAGAS faithfulness is unreliable" as supported only in the general form given by Verify with Caution + TRIVIA+.

### Implementation ideas, ranked

**1. Fix the DAG's fabrication at its actual root — one expression.** `orchestrator/execution_manager.py:762`:

```python
fields = node.output_contract.produced_fields if node.output_contract else ["result"]
produced_outputs = {field: response_text for field in fields}   # every field = the same blob
```

A node declaring `produced_fields = ["hypotheses", "evidence", "risks"]` gets all three set to the identical full response, and `validate_outputs` (`contracts.py:97`) passes because it only checks presence plus primitive type. That is a **100% schema-validity, ~0% field-fidelity** system — the exact pathology *The Constraint Tax* quantifies (validity 61.5%→100.0% while accuracy 19.7%→11.0% and wrong-valid-schema 49.5%→88.9%). This single expression is why "the DAG executor fabricates outputs"; it is not a planner problem. Fix: require the node to emit a JSON object keyed by `produced_fields`, parse it, fail the node on a missing key. Corollary bug: because every value is a `str`, any contract declaring `dict`/`list`/`integer` types fails type validation, so the DAG only functions today when all fields are untyped or `string`.

**2. Replace `DEFAULT_MAX_REPAIRS = 2` with a stability-gated adaptive stop** (`repair.py:68`). Two independent results converge: VRR-Stop beat fixed five-round repair by **+60.6 pp true validity at 0.72 average rounds**, and *Self-Correction as Feedback Control* gives the closed-form gate **iterate only when ECR/EIR > Acc/(1−Acc)**, under which most of 7 tested models were net-negative refiners (GPT-5 and four others lost accuracy; only o3-mini clearly gained, +3.4 pp). Measure EIR/ECR per model tier offline, store beside the tier config, and let `run_repair_loop` refuse to iterate for tiers below threshold. Cap at 3 rounds, not 2, for tiers that pass.

**3. Make `disagreement_delta` semantic instead of lexical.** `consensus.py:160` `_pairwise_agreement` is Jaccard over claim-string sets: two agents agreeing in substance but differing in phrasing register as maximal disagreement, and two agreeing on identical wrong wording register as consensus. Swap in embedding similarity and expose TU = AU + EU, where EU = inter-model minus intra-model sequence-semantic similarity (5 models, 10 long-form tasks) — EU specifically catches confident-wrong cases. Note `risk_score`/`disagreement_delta`/`judge_select` currently live **only** in `.claude/worktrees/aetheris-pipeline-hardening-2026-08-10/`, not on `main`; reconcile before building on them.

**4. Add a graph spectral pre-flight check (~30 lines).** In `reasoning_graph.py`: build the row-stochastic operator P from the TaskGraph, compute M = (I − γP)⁻¹, reject plans whose κ(M) exceeds a threshold. κ(M) rank-predicted perturbation robustness at Spearman **r_s = 1.0** across chain/star/mesh, and it costs no tokens because it is a property of the graph alone. Record ρ(M) but **do not** trust its sign (r_s = −1.0, perfectly inverted).

**5. Move escalation from per-request to per-node.** Difficulty shifts mid-rollout; step-level risk-calibrated escalation delivered **94.3% on HumanEval+ at 0.60% escalation** and **98.2% on TextWorld (from 64.6% SLM-only) at 41.7%**. AETHERIS decides tier once, at the Breaker Gate. Add an escalation hook inside `execute_node` (`execution_manager.py`), thresholded on residual failures already in `experience_db.py`.

**6. Add the missing lower threshold to the Breaker Gate.** *Conformal Thinking* pairs an upper "confident, stop" threshold with a **parametric lower threshold that abandons apparently-unsolvable instances**, and attributes its compute savings mainly to that lower bound. Today the gate detects knowledge absence but still runs the pipeline. Give it a calibrated give-up exit returning an honest abstention.

**7. Promote `execution_replay.py` from telemetry to source of truth.** `ReplayRecorder.emit` / `ExecutionTrace` / `ReplayStore` already implement the Managed Agents *Session* interface (`emitEvent`/`getEvents`) — an append-only log outside the harness, whose stated payoff is "nothing in the harness needs to survive a crash" plus **p50 TTFT ~60% / p95 >90%** reductions from lazy provisioning. Add a `wake(trace_id)` equivalent that reconstructs state from events; that is what turns `replay_trace(mode="simulate")` into recovery. And per Temporal — "the checkpoint preserves your data, not your execution" — you also need a supervisor that decides the re-entry point (`background_tasks.py`).

**8. Bound what crosses each stage boundary.** A sub-agent may burn tens of thousands of tokens and return **1,000–2,000 tokens** of distilled summary. Add a token ceiling to `OutputContract` in `contracts.py` so Logician/Creative hand the Judge distilled claims, not transcripts. Independently: never re-inject a failed output verbatim on retry — self-conditioning on one's own errors raises further error probability.

**9. Instrument the four metrics that distinguish valid from correct.** `metrics.py` should track schema validity, answer accuracy, executable accuracy, and **wrong-valid-schema rate** separately; today only the first is reportable. Also `actual_tokens = max(1, len(response_text) // 4)` (`execution_manager.py:755`) is a character-count estimate feeding `PipelineBudget` — replace with provider usage or the budget is fiction.

**10. Close the routing loop instead of extending the regex table.** `routing.py` `_ROUTE_PATTERNS` is hand-written regex. RouteNLP's production numbers (58% cost cut, 91% acceptance, p99 1,847 ms → 387 ms over an 8-week / 5K-queries-per-day pilot; 40–85% cost reduction at 96–100% structured quality) came from clustering escalation failures and retraining the router, with targeted distillation worth **>2×** untargeted. `experience_db.py` already holds the raw material.

### Verdict: should the dormant DAG runtime be revived or deleted?

**Split it: revive the substrate, hard-delete the planner ambition. Fix the one-line fabrication bug first, because a contract layer that certifies fabricated content is strictly worse than no contract layer.**

- **Scale says DAG is the right paradigm *for AETHERIS's size*.** The only head-to-head 2026 study verified here (2606.20058, 208 production-derived scenarios) found DAG Plan-and-Execute "offers higher precision and structured parallelization at smaller scales, but its higher overhead worsens at enterprise scale; ReAct is more robust." Their small scale was <10 agents; the crossover appeared at 200. `_NODE_CAP_BY_COMPLEXITY` is 5/8/12/16 — the regime where DAG wins — and the paper's dominant failure mode at scale (agent-discovery noise) does not apply to a fixed 4-role pipeline. Deleting the DAG on "reactive agents won" grounds would be importing someone else's scale conditions.
- **Security independently favours a pre-committed plan.** Plan-then-execute means untrusted content can affect data values and branch selection but "cannot redefine the user task or cause the model to synthesize new actions at runtime" (100% of analyzed WebArena tasks compatible, 80% needing no runtime LLM step). For a system whose Breaker Gate ingests untrusted queries, freezing the plan and hash-pinning it into the ExecutionManifest is a real control.
- **But the current implementation delivers none of the DAG's value and all of its cost.** Every declared output field gets the same response string; the validator checks presence plus primitive type; token accounting is `len//4`. So there is no genuine stage isolation, no field-level contract, and no real budget enforcement. MAST finds failures concentrate in system design and **task verification**, not model capability; SEMAP finds a protocol layer of contracts + structured messaging + lifecycle verification cuts MAST failures by **up to 69.6%**. Both locate the payoff in the verification layer. AETHERIS built the graph and stubbed the verification.
- **Topology evidence says do not grow the graph.** Per-agent degree 2 gives mean first-generation error offspring **0.667 (<1, errors die out)**; degree 4 and 5 give **1.333 and 1.667 (>1, sustained propagation)** across 21,000 simulated trajectories. Moderate sparsity is optimal. Unguided homogeneous debate costs **2.1–3.4× tokens for equal-or-worse accuracy** with sycophantic adoption up to **85.5%**. A richer reasoning graph is a measurable downgrade.
- **The ceiling may already be reached.** Any pick-one policy is capped at 1−β, and "combining models rarely beats the single best model without a strong query-level routing signal" (67 models). Anthropic's own 90.2% multi-agent win was **80% explained by token usage alone**. Measure β before reviving anything that adds nodes.
- **Anthropic's own trajectory is the clincher on the planner.** Their Labs harness went from initializer+coder to a three-agent system with sprint contracts (16 features / 10 sprints, 27 criteria in one sprint; 6 hr / $200 vs 20 min / $9 solo, >20× cost) and then, on Opus 4.6, **dropped sprint decomposition entirely** because the builder held coherence for two hours unaided — "every component in a harness encodes an assumption about what the model can't do on its own." `execution_planner.py` + `strategic_planner.py` + `reasoning_graph.py` encode assumptions about 2025-era models; their expected lifetime is short.

**Concrete verdict.**

- **Keep and revive:** `contracts.py` (make validation semantic), `execution_replay.py` (promote to source of truth), `checkpoints.py` (add exit/async/sync modes + per-node pending writes), `budget.py` (real token accounting), `uncertainty.py`, `repair.py` (adaptive stop), `resource_manager.py`. These are the reliability substrate and they pay off on the *live* 4-stage pipeline whether or not a DAG ever ships.
- **Freeze behind a flag you never intend to flip, or delete:** general-purpose DAG execution in `execution_planner.py` / `reasoning_graph.py` / `scheduler.py`; `meta_reasoner.py` mid-run graph mutation (mutation + contracts + replay is three interacting sources of nondeterminism); `prediction.py`; `skills.py` dynamic dispatch.
- **The honest reframing:** AETHERIS's live pipeline *is already a 4-node DAG*, hand-written in `pipelines.py`. Revive the runtime only far enough to express that fixed shape with real contracts, real budgets, and real replay. Do not revive the ability to *generate* arbitrary graphs. That is the version the 2026 evidence supports, and a much smaller diff than either full revival or full deletion.

### Cheap wins (≤1 day each)

1. **Measure β.** Log per-agent correctness on your eval set, compute the all-wrong rate with a Clopper–Pearson upper bound. One afternoon, and it tells you whether *any* orchestration work can pay off.
2. **Two-framing abstention in the Breaker Gate.** Ask the same question Direct and Incremental with the same model; abstain on disagreement. Beats standard uncertainty baselines on F1 and AUROC; no retrieval, no fine-tune, no second provider.
3. **Swap Jaccard for embedding similarity** in `consensus.py:160`. Same signature, correct signal.
4. **Raise `DEFAULT_MAX_REPAIRS` 2 → 3 and log realized round counts.** Cheapest half of the repair fix; the adaptive stop can follow.
5. **Stop re-injecting failed output on retry** — pass the defect description only. Direct mitigation of self-conditioning.
6. **Add a wrong-valid-schema counter** to `metrics.py`: contract-valid outputs the firewall later rejects. This is the number that would have surfaced the DAG fabrication bug years earlier.
7. **Replace `actual_tokens = len(response_text) // 4`** (`execution_manager.py:755`) with provider usage. The budget currently enforces a fiction.
8. **κ(M) pre-flight check** in `reasoning_graph.py` — ~30 lines of NumPy, zero token cost, r_s = 1.0 against perturbation robustness.
9. **Cap Logician/Creative → Judge payload at ~2k tokens** of distilled claims via `OutputContract`.
10. **Tag MAST failure modes on `ReplayEvent`** — 14 modes, 3 categories, an enum and a field.
11. **Reconcile the stray worktree.** `risk_score` / `disagreement_delta` / `judge_select` exist only in `.claude/worktrees/aetheris-pipeline-hardening-2026-08-10/orchestrator/{evaluation,pipelines}.py`, not on `main`. Merge or drop.
12. **Per-tier "think before JSON" flag.** Frontier closed models show little format tax; open-weight tiers need freeform-then-reformat, which recovers most of the loss.

### Unverified / rejected (Angle 2)

**Promising but unverified — do not cite until fetched.** *Capable language models can outgrow the benefits of collaboration*, Nature Machine Intelligence [s42256-026-01268-y](https://www.nature.com/articles/s42256-026-01268-y) — fetch returned a **303 redirect to an auth wall**; the snippet claim ("single-agent baseline performance emerges as the most robust predictor of whether coordination improves or decreases performance") is a highly relevant negative result but title, authors and numbers are unconfirmed. *LLM-Planned Execution Graphs (BatchDAG)* [2607.18241](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18241) — snippet gives BatchDAG **3.74/5** vs expert pipeline 3.25/5 vs ReAct 3.09/5, p<0.05, on 12 queries; directly on-point for revive-vs-delete, but abs page not fetched and n=12 is thin. **Partly resolved by the 2026-08-21 audit: the ID resolves, real title *BatchDAG: LLM-Planned Execution Graphs for Scalable Ad-Hoc Analysis Over Enterprise Data*, published 2026-04-17 — a 75-day gap from the `2607` prefix, the largest in the whole 147-ID set. The three scores are still NOT in the abstract, so this stays unverified for numbers.** Numeric result tables for *Predictive Maps* v2, *Conformal Thinking*, *Conformal Cascade*, *Adaptive Test-Time Compute* live only in the PDFs. Anthropic *When AI Builds Itself* (June 2026, referenced secondhand) — no first-party URL located; treat as nonexistent. tianpan.co's "95% per-step → 36% over 20 steps" — arithmetic checks out (0.95²⁰ ≈ 0.358) and it's a useful framing, but cite 2509.09677 or 2608.06663 instead. Chroma "Context Rot" — referenced by verified sources (Anthropic, Cognition), primary report not located.

**Rejected.** All SEO/aggregator framework comparisons (markaicode, digitalapplied, letsdatascience, slashdev, uvik, raftlabs, requesty, effloow) — confident 2026 claims ("AutoGen is in maintenance mode", "CrewAI crossed 2 billion agentic executions", "LangGraph 1.1", "SOC 2 Type II") with no primary sourcing and mutually inconsistent details; use the LangGraph and OpenAI Agents SDK docs instead. Medium/Substack retellings of Anthropic's 90.2% — superseded by the first-party post. MDPI *The Selection Bottleneck in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines* (Applied Sciences 16/10/4914) — relevant framing (heterogeneous MoA vs Self-MoA) but covered rigorously with numbers by 2606.27288 and 2605.01566. MDPI USV swarm topology (71.7% comms overhead reduction) — robotics swarm control, out of scope. Surfaced but not fetched, therefore not cited: 2607.26922, 2603.04421 (**audit 2026-08-21: resolves as *Do Mixed-Vendor Multi-Agent LLMs Improve Clinical Diagnosis?*, published 2026-02-14 — 15 days before the `2603` prefix**), 2605.24202, 2509.23537, 2506.06843, 2511.10037, 2604.13888, 2601.07143, 2604.00510, 2604.14853, 2509.25420, 2603.20895, 2606.22840, 2410.10347, **2406.18665 (RouteLLM — canonical router baseline)**, **2406.06461 (budget-aware evaluation; snippet claims debate/Reflexion can *worsen* with more budget)**, 2511.02603, 2509.14004, 2506.02536, 2305.11860, 2601.07525, 2605.02363, 2604.14862, **2606.21724 (DISC structured verification loops)**, 2608.05917, 2607.05775, 2604.08588, 2604.04269, 2510.07799, 2602.11897, 2603.24481, 2605.09618, 2605.27768, 2605.05812 — the bolded four are the obvious next reading list.

---

## Angle 3 — Security & trust boundaries

**Scoping note that drives every ranking below: AETHERIS is not a tool-using agent.** Its agents call LLM providers and return text — no shell, no filesystem writes, no MCP client, no browser. [`orchestrator/skills.py`](../orchestrator/skills.py) is a hardcoded `_BUILTIN_SKILLS` dict with no `exec`/`eval`/`importlib`/`subprocess`. So CaMeL / dual-LLM / plan-then-execute buy AETHERIS almost nothing — there are no privileged tool calls to gate. The live trust boundaries are exactly five:

1. model output → React renderer
2. **upstream model output re-fed as prompt text** at [`execution_manager.py:721-725`](../orchestrator/execution_manager.py)
3. unauthenticated-adjacent HTTP surface + cost DoS
4. pip dependency floors
5. JWT lifecycle

Verification for this angle used `arxiv.org/abs/…` page fetches (the arXiv API is blocked from this environment — a control query with three known-good IDs returned an empty body, confirming the block rather than missing records), plus the **OSV API** (`api.osv.dev`) and **NVD 2.0 API** (`services.nvd.nist.gov`) directly for every CVE, version range, severity and publication date. No vendor blog was used as a source of numbers.

### Verified sources

| **FastAPI / SQLAlchemy / httpx / pydantic clean** | [OSV](https://api.osv.dev/v1/query) | four OSV queries | FastAPI: only **CVE-2021-32677** (fixed 0.65.2) plus the transitive python-multipart entry — nothing 2024-2026. SQLAlchemy: nothing after **2019** (CVE-2019-7548). httpx: nothing after **CVE-2021-41945** (fixed 0.23.0). pydantic: nothing after **CVE-2024-3772** ReDoS (fixed 2.4.0) | Reconfirms the prior audit. Risk is entirely in `python-jose` + `starlette`/`python-multipart` floors | — | — | — |

| **CVE-2026-22813** — model output → markdown renderer → XSS → RCE | [GHSA-c83v-7274-4vgp](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-c83v-7274-4vgp) | OSV `/v1/vulns/CVE-2026-22813` (sourced from CVEProject/cvelistV5) + OSV npm query returning the full GHSA text. CWE-79, published 2026-01-12, fixed **1.1.10** | "The markdown renderer used for LLM responses will insert arbitrary HTML into the DOM. **There is no sanitization with DOMPurify or even a CSP** on the web interface… controlling the LLM response for a chat session gets JavaScript execution on the `http://localhost:4096` origin", then RCE via same-origin `fetch()` to `/pty/` | The exact shape of the risk in `aetheris-ui/src/components/MessageBubble.jsx:264-271` (react-markdown) + `:100-103` (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML`). That tree is gitignored and **not served** — `server.py:250` serves `frontend/dist`, whose `FormattedMessage.jsx` is pure JSX with zero HTML sinks. Keep it that way and add a CSP so a future renderer swap can't become this | S | L | **H** |
| **CVE-2026-22812** — unauthenticated local server + permissive CORS | [NVD](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-22812) | NVD API: **CVSS 8.8 HIGH**, published 2026-01-12, status Analyzed, fixed 1.0.216 | "OpenCode automatically starts an unauthenticated HTTP server that allows any local process (**or any website via permissive CORS**) to execute arbitrary shell commands with the user's privileges" | AETHERIS is on the right side of this: `_resolve_cors_origins()` (`server.py:103-116`) rejects `*` and requires ≥1 explicit origin, and a CSRF `Origin`/`Referer` check runs on all mutating methods. Worth an explicit regression test so nobody "fixes" CORS to `*` while debugging | S | L | M |
| **CVE-2025-32711 (EchoLeak)** — zero-click exfil from a production LLM app | [NVD](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-32711) | NVD API: published 2025-06-11, **CVSS 3.1 = 9.3 CRITICAL** (`AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:L/A:N`), description "Ai command injection in M365 Copilot allows an unauthorized attacker to disclose information over a network." Not in OSV (Microsoft CVEs aren't) | Highest-severity real-world proof that indirect injection → data disclosure is a shipped-product bug class, not a lab result | Justification for the CSP/egress work rather than a technical control of its own | — | — | — |
| **OWASP LLM10:2026** — Improper Output Handling | [raw md](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GenAI-Security-Project/GenAI-LLM-Top10/main/2026/final/LLM10_ImproperOutputHandling.md) | fetched raw; risk list and mitigation list quoted verbatim | Risk #7: "The chat UI auto-renders Markdown images or link previews referenced in model output, allowing an attacker who controls part of the model context to **exfiltrate conversation data via the image URL's hostname or query string**". Risk factor: "Client renderers… that automatically fetch external resources". Mitigation #6: "Employ **strict Content Security Policies (CSP)**". Mitigation #8: strip ANSI/BEL/OSC/backspace/CR before writing model output to terminals or logs | Two concrete gaps: (a) **no CSP anywhere** — `frontend/index.html` has no meta CSP and `server.py` has exactly one `add_middleware` call (CORS, `:242`); a grep for `Content-Security-Policy\|X-Frame-Options\|Strict-Transport\|X-Content-Type-Options\|Referrer-Policy\|TrustedHost` across all `*.py` hits only that line. (b) `main.py`'s CLI REPL prints model output straight to a terminal with no control-character stripping | S | L | **H** |
| **OWASP LLM06:2026** — Unbounded Consumption | [raw md](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GenAI-Security-Project/GenAI-LLM-Top10/main/2026/final/LLM06_UnboundedConsumption.md) | fetched raw; mitigations #1, #2, #4, #9 quoted verbatim | "Traditional request-rate limiting alone is no longer sufficient." Required: **tokens-per-minute and tokens-per-day limits, pre-flight token estimation to reject before inference**, "**non-overridable budget ceilings per API key, user, team**… enforcement mechanisms that halt inference when exceeded, rather than alerting thresholds", **agentic circuit breakers** (step/recursion/time/per-run cost ceilings, state hashing for loops), and sandboxing to restrict network reach | The sharpest live gap. `api_gateway/rate_limiter.py` already implements `TokenBucket` with `tokens_per_minute` and a `user_limits: dict[str, TokenBucket]` — but the only production caller, `core/runtime.py:331-332`, passes `provider="default", user_id=user_id` where `user_id` is never populated. **`user_limits` stays empty and every request shares one bucket**, so `/api/query` and `/api/query/stream` have no effective per-user or per-IP limit; only `/auth/*` is limited (5/min, in-process dict, `server.py:73-88`). With 9 live provider keys behind it, that is an unmetered denial-of-wallet path | S→M | L | **H** |

| **OWASP LLM04:2026** — Supply Chain | [raw md](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/GenAI-Security-Project/GenAI-LLM-Top10/main/2026/final/LLM04_SupplyChain.md) | fetched raw | Mitigation #2: keep the app "on maintained versions of components"; **"verify that AI-suggested dependencies exist and are the intended package"**. #4: signed SBOM/AIBOM (CycloneDX ML-BOM). #5: signing + transparency log (OpenSSF Model Signing, Sigstore) — "**Signing proves integrity and origin, not safety**"; combine with immutable refs and SLSA | `bcrypt` is imported directly at `core/security.py:14` but never declared — it arrives transitively via `passlib[bcrypt]`. `testcontainers[postgres]` (test-only) sits in the runtime file. Nothing is pinned or hashed | S | L | H |
| **pip hash-checking mode** | [pip secure installs](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/topics/secure-installs/) | fetched; flag names and the three requirements quoted | "Specifying `--hash` against **any** requirement will activate this mode globally." `--require-hashes` forces it. "Hashes are required for **all** requirements" **and** "for **all** dependencies". "Requirements must be pinned (either to a URL, filesystem path or using `==`)." Recommends `sha256` + `--only-binary :all:`; md5/sha1/sha224 rejected. `--no-require-hashes` added in pip 26.2 | Every one of AETHERIS's 19 runtime requirements uses `>=`; there is **no lockfile at all** (`uv.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `constraints.txt`, `pyproject.toml` all absent). Generate a hashed constraints file and add `--require-hashes` to the CI install step | S | L | **H** |
| **PEP 740** — index-hosted attestations | [peps.python.org/pep-0740](https://peps.python.org/pep-0740/) | fetched; "Status: Final", created 08-Jan-2024, resolved 17-Jul-2024, authors Woodruff/Tuesca/Ingram | Attestation v1 pinned to one suite: X.509 (RFC 5280) + **ECDSA P-256/SHA-256**, payload = in-toto v1 Statement signed with **DSSE** (`application/vnd.in-toto+json`), subject digest must include SHA-256 hex. Predicates: **SLSA Provenance v1** and **PyPI Publish Attestation v1**. Identity from **Trusted Publishing**; verification material = cert + transparency-log inclusion proofs (Sigstore shape, though the text doesn't name Sigstore). Explicitly makes no policy recommendation that installers verify | Ceiling on what pip pinning can prove today: hashes give integrity against tampering, not provenance. Note it; don't build on it yet | — | — | L |

The old `/specs/semconv/gen-ai/` page is a bare relocation notice | Names to adopt: `gen_ai.provider.name` (supersedes `gen_ai.system`), `gen_ai.operation.name` (`chat`, `execute_tool`, `invoke_agent`, `retrieval`, …), `gen_ai.request.model`, `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` / `.output_tokens` (supersede `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens`), `gen_ai.conversation.id`, `gen_ai.agent.{id,name}`, `gen_ai.tool.*`, `gen_ai.retrieval.documents`, `gen_ai.usage.cache_read.input_tokens`. `gen_ai.prompt`/`gen_ai.completion` **removed with no replacement**. `gen_ai.input.messages`/`.output_messages` carry an explicit "likely to contain sensitive information including user/PII data" warning; instrumentations "MAY provide a way for users to filter or truncate" | `telemetry/observer.py:64-66` logs `[METRIC] Model: … Tokens: I=…/O=… Cost: $…` as a free-text string. Rename to the semconv attribute keys so it is queryable, and treat `AETHERIS_LOG_MODEL_IO` as the semconv content-capture opt-in it effectively is.
