| Artificial Analysis × NVIDIA guardrail benchmark | [article](https://artificialanalysis.ai/articles/guardrail-safety-benchmark) | fetched. Published **11 Jun 2026**. Protocol verified; **per-model F1 and latency live in interactive charts and were not extractable — no per-model numbers from this source** | 20 models (Llama Guard 3 8B / 4 12B, Qwen3Guard Gen 8B, Nemotron Content Safety 3/3.5, WildGuard, Granite Guardian 4.1 8B think/no-think, ShieldGemma 9B, gpt-oss-safeguard-20B/120B, omni-moderation). **7,232 prompts**: WildGuardTest 1,699 + ToxicChat 5,083 (~7% toxic) + XSTest 450 (250 safe / 200 unsafe). Metric = equal-weight mean of 3 dataset F1s. Latency: self-hosted B200, 1 request in flight, 20 warmup discarded, **450 timed requests**, temp 0, non-streaming. **PromptGuard was not in this run**; gpt-oss rows are "exploratory prompted baselines" | Use the protocol if you ever benchmark a local guard. Do **not** quote accuracy or latency from it — none was verifiable | — | — | — |

### Implementation ideas, ranked

Ordered defense-in-depth: blast-radius containment first (survives adaptive attackers), detection last (degrades against them) — the explicit ordering logic in OWASP LLM01:2026.

**1. Security-headers + CSP middleware — `server.py`, next to the CORS block at `:242`.** The only `add_middleware` call in the codebase is CORS; there is no CSP, HSTS, `X-Content-Type-Options`, frame-deny, or referrer policy, and `frontend/index.html` has no meta CSP either. One middleware, ~15 lines:

```python
# after the CORS middleware at server.py:242
CSP = (
    "default-src 'self'; "
    "img-src 'self' data:; "          # kills markdown-image / pixel exfil (OWASP LLM10 risk #7)
    "connect-src 'self'; "            # kills fetch/beacon exfil from injected script
    "script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; "
    "font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; "
    "object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'"
)

@app.middleware("http")
async def security_headers(request, call_next):
    r = await call_next(request)
    r.headers.setdefault("Content-Security-Policy", CSP)
    r.headers.setdefault("X-Content-Type-Options", "nosniff")
    r.headers.setdefault("Referrer-Policy", "no-referrer")
    r.headers.setdefault("X-Frame-Options", "DENY")
    if settings.ENVIRONMENT != "development":
        r.headers.setdefault("Strict-Transport-Security", "max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains")
    return r
```

`frontend/index.html` loads `fonts.googleapis.com`, so add `style-src https://fonts.googleapis.com` or self-host the font and tighten to `style-src 'self'`. Also add `TrustedHostMiddleware` while you are there. Cited by: OWASP LLM10 mitigation #6, CVE-2026-22813 ("no sanitization with DOMPurify **or even a CSP**"). **Effort S · Risk L · ROI H.**

**2. Activate the per-user token bucket that already exists — `core/runtime.py:331-332` + `server.py:496/564`.** `api_gateway/rate_limiter.py` has `user_limits`, `tokens_per_minute`, `TokenBucket.consume`, `time_until_tokens` — all of it unreachable because `user_id` is never populated. Thread `current_user.email` (or id) from the route through `execution_manager.execute` into `acquire_resources(provider=..., user_id=..., tokens=estimated)`. Then add what OWASP LLM06 actually asks for: pre-flight token estimation (`tiktoken` is already a dependency) to reject before inference, a tokens-per-day bucket, and a **non-overridable** per-user daily cost ceiling wired to `telemetry/observer.py`'s `MODEL_PRICING` that *halts* rather than alerts. Known ceiling: buckets are in-process `dict`s, so limits are per-worker and reset on restart — fine for a single uvicorn worker, needs Redis the day you scale out. **Effort S→M · Risk L · ROI H.**

**3. Upgrade the two dependency floors that are actually exploitable — `requirements.txt`.** `starlette>=1.6.0` (clears CVE-2026-48818 UNC SSRF/NTLM on Windows — HIGH 7.5, directly applicable given `StaticFiles` on `frontend/dist` on Windows 11 — plus 48710/48817/54283/62727/54121) and `python-multipart>=0.0.32` (clears 8 advisories). Declare `bcrypt` explicitly; move `testcontainers[postgres]` to `requirements-dev.txt`. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI H.**

**4. Replace python-jose with PyJWT and add the missing claims — `core/security.py:324-336` and `:356`.** python-jose has had no release since 3.5.0 (2025-05-28), carries CVE-2025-61152 (`alg=none`, CVSS 6.5) **with no patched version**, and FastAPI's own docs moved back to PyJWT in PR #11589. Pin `PyJWT==2.13.0` (the CVE-2026-48522 fix). The API is nearly drop-in; while you are in there:

```python
# mint (replaces core/security.py:324-336)
now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
to_encode.update({
    "iat": now, "nbf": now, "exp": expire,
    "jti": secrets.token_urlsafe(16),
    "iss": "aetheris", "aud": "aetheris-api",
})

# verify (replaces core/security.py:356)
payload = jwt.decode(
    token, settings.JWT_SECRET_KEY,
    algorithms=[settings.JWT_ALGORITHM],      # keep: this is what neuters alg=none
    audience="aetheris-api", issuer="aetheris",
    leeway=30,                                 # clock skew; jose default is 0
    options={"require": ["exp", "iat", "sub", "jti"]},
)
if await denylist.contains(payload["jti"]):
    raise credentials_exception
```

`jti` is what makes revocation possible at all: today `POST /auth/logout` (`server.py:473`) only deletes the cookie, so a stolen token stays valid for the full 60 minutes. The denylist can be a table in the Postgres already running — TTL = `JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES`. RFC 9700 §2.3 requires that a resource server **MUST** refuse a token whose audience doesn't match, which is only enforceable once `aud` exists. Also fix the lowercase aliases `aetheris_JWT_ALGORITHM` / `aetheris_JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES` (`core/config.py:218,224`) — with `case_sensitive=True` those two settings cannot be overridden by their documented uppercase env names. **Effort M · Risk M · ROI H.**

**5. Hash-pinned dependency install in CI.** `pip freeze` → `constraints.txt` with `==`, then `pip-compile --generate-hashes` (or `uv pip compile --generate-hashes`), and install with `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock --only-binary :all:`. pip's own docs are explicit that hashes are required for *all* requirements *and* all transitive dependencies, and that everything must be pinned with `==`. PEP 740 attestations are Final and PyPI-implemented but installers don't verify them yet — hashes are the enforceable layer today. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI H.**

**6. Deterministic gate on the agent→agent channel — `orchestrator/execution_manager.py:721-725`.** The highest-value *injection* control in the codebase, and the one paper that matches AETHERIS's architecture exactly. The current code:

```python
prompt = (
    f"User request:\n{user_query}\n\n"
    f"Current task:\n{node.objective}\n\n"
    f"Upstream results:\n{json.dumps(upstream, default=str)}"
)
```

`upstream` is prior **model output** re-fed as instructions-adjacent text with plain labels and no boundary check — ChannelGuard's exact threat model, where an embedding-similarity information-bottleneck gate blocked tool poisoning 30/30 and halved prompt-injection ASR (0.333 → 0.167) with **no extra LLM call** and no accuracy loss (GSM8K 0.867 unchanged). Implement as: embed each upstream field, cosine against a small adversarial phrase bank, then pass / compress / block. Log the decision with the `request_id`. Known ceiling, straight from the paper: white-box adaptive paraphrase evades every embedding gate — so this reduces ASR, it does not close the channel. Pair it with the Map-Reduce pattern from arXiv:2506.08837: constrain what a downstream node can consume from upstream (a score, an enum, a bounded summary) rather than free text. **Effort M · Risk L · ROI H.**

**7. Data-marking at the one chokepoint — `api_gateway/client.py:51`.** Every agent call funnels through `messages.append({"role": "user", "content": prompt})`, and the comment right above it at `:46-51` already *claims* "AsyncAPIGateway validates and JSON-escapes user-controlled prompts before they reach this network boundary" — which is false. Worse, the functions to make it true are already written and never called: `core/security.py:270-275 escape_user_input` and `:277-289 separate_system_user_prompts` (grep finds callers only in `tests/test_security.py`). Wrap untrusted spans with a per-request random nonce delimiter (`<untrusted id="{nonce}">…</untrusted>`) so the marking cannot be mimicked by content that already saw the scheme. Set expectations honestly from the measurements: spotlighting's original >50% → <2% is a 2024 GPT-family result that **does not replicate** — in 2026 it merely halves ASR on Claude Haiku and gives **no benefit** on Llama 3.1 8B, and StruQ-style provenance labeling was bypassed under adaptive attack. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI M.**

**8. Request-scoped audit logging — one middleware in `server.py`.** Today `request_id` is threaded manually via `extra={...}` at roughly a dozen call sites (`core/runtime.py:321,350,423,463`; `orchestrator/checkpoints.py:146,178,273`; `orchestrator/state_machine.py:157,198,230,257`), there is **no `structlog.contextvars.bind_contextvars` anywhere**, and no HTTP access log — so most lines carry no correlation id and there is no record of who called what. One middleware calling `bind_contextvars(request_id=..., user=..., route=...)` puts the id on every line for free, and deletes all twelve manual `extra=` blocks. Add auth-event logging (login success/failure, role denials at `core/security.py:373-392`) — none exists today. Adopt the OTel GenAI attribute names in `telemetry/observer.py` (`gen_ai.provider.name`, `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens`, `gen_ai.conversation.id`) instead of the `[METRIC] …` free-text string. Also the cheapest hedge against EU AI Act Art. 12, which requires "automatic recording of events (logs) over the lifetime of the system" and applies from 2 Aug 2026 for in-scope high-risk systems. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI M.**

**9. Drop the silent live-keys override — `secrets_bootstrap.py:151-152`.** Loading any keyring key auto-sets `os.environ["AETHERIS_ALLOW_LIVE_KEYS"] = "1"`, which disables the leaked-prefix guard at `core/config.py:71-80` for the whole process. That guard's own docstring describes it as an explicit operator opt-in that is never silent. Delete the two lines; make the operator set it. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI M.**

**10. `logs/model_io.log` — 6,096,678 bytes of plaintext prompts and responses on disk.** Gitignored and `scrub_secrets`-filtered, but unrotated and uncapped, and its size proves `AETHERIS_LOG_MODEL_IO` has been run enabled. Add a `RotatingFileHandler` with a byte cap and retention, and treat it as the semconv-sensitive content channel it is. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI M.**

**11. `skills-lock.json`: verify the hash or delete the file.** It stores a `computedHash` per skill (`sha256`, GitHub `sourceType`), and grep for `skills-lock|computedHash` across the whole repo matches **only `.gitignore`** — nothing verifies it. `skills/` also contains executables (`skills/anti-slop/scripts/clean_slop.py`, `skills/impeccable/scripts/*.mjs`) that no AETHERIS code path touches. Per CoSAI's tiers this is L2 without the check; per OWASP LLM01 #10, "Pinning does not stop a payload shipped in the pinned version". Since these are Claude Code agent skills and not AETHERIS runtime, deleting the untracked file is the honest fix. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI M.**

**12. Explicit egress allowlist + split timeouts — `api_gateway/client.py:24` and `:77-108`.** The hardcoded provider→URL map is already a de facto allowlist that raises `ValueError` on an unknown prefix, and **no URL anywhere is derived from model output, retrieved documents, or the request body — there is no SSRF sink.** Two improvements: hoist the map into a named `ALLOWED_HOSTS` constant so the property is testable rather than incidental, and replace the flat `httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=600.0)` with `httpx.Timeout(connect=5, read=600, write=30, pool=5)` plus `limits=` — a flat 600 s connect timeout is a self-inflicted resource-exhaustion amplifier under LLM06. **Effort S · Risk L · ROI M.**

**13. Do NOT build an inline injection classifier as a gate.** The numbers say it fails: PromptGuard 2 detects **37.3%** of indirect injections, LlamaGuard **27.4%**, LLM-as-judge **7.1%**; known-answer detection can be driven to **0% detection at 91% ASR** (DataFlip); adaptive attacks beat 12 recent defenses at **>90%**. Activation probes are the only thing that works well (68.0% indirect / 99.0% agentic at 6.5% FPR, <1 ms) and they need hidden states AETHERIS does not have over HTTP. Keep the 11 regexes at `core/security.py:81-93` as **alerting and telemetry**, not as an authorization decision — and note that benchmark AUCs are inflated 8.0–16.5 points by standard cross-validation, so any vendor number quoted is optimistic. **A decision, not a change.**

**14. Paraphrase retrieved snippets — only when RAG lights up.** `orchestrator/retrieval.py:171-180` `DeterministicRetrievalProvider` returns `[]` and is the default; `ContextWindow.retrieved_snippets` is consumed only by tests, so **no retrieved text reaches any prompt today**. When `AETHERIS_ENABLE_RAG` flips on, wrap at `orchestrator/context_manager.py:377-381` where `content=source.excerpt or source.title or source.url` is built. Paraphrasing beat every other prompting defense at **55–84% ASR reduction** and outperformed a Llama Guard 4 config on all three models tested — one cheap model call per snippet, and it dominates delimiters. **Effort M (deferred) · Risk M · ROI H when RAG ships.**

### Cheap wins (≤1 day each)

- **CSP + 4 security headers middleware** at `server.py:242` (~15 lines). Biggest single risk reduction in the repo.
- **`starlette>=1.6.0`** in `requirements.txt` — one line clears a HIGH-severity Windows SSRF/NTLM-theft path that applies to the `StaticFiles` mount today. Add `python-multipart>=0.0.32`.
- **Delete `python-jose`'s `>=3.3.0` floor.** Even before the PyJWT migration, `python-jose[cryptography]>=3.5.0` removes two 2024 CVEs (one CRITICAL) from the resolvable set.
- **`options={"require": [...]}` + `leeway=30` + `iat`/`jti`** on the existing jose calls (`core/security.py:324-336`, `:356`). Works in jose; makes the later PyJWT swap mechanical.
- **Pass `user_id` into `acquire_resources`** (`core/runtime.py:331-332`) — a one-line plumbing fix that activates an entire per-user rate limiter currently sitting as dead code.
- **Delete `secrets_bootstrap.py:151-152`** (silent `AETHERIS_ALLOW_LIVE_KEYS=1`).
- **Delete the false comment at `api_gateway/client.py:46-51`** — it documents escaping that doesn't happen, which is worse than no comment. Or make it true by calling `escape_user_input`, which is already written.
- **Rotate/cap `logs/model_io.log`** (currently ~6 MB of plaintext prompts, no cap).
- **`--require-hashes` constraints file** generated once and wired into the CI install step.
- **structlog `bind_contextvars` middleware** — puts `request_id` on every log line and lets you delete ~12 manual `extra={}` blocks.
- **Log auth events** (login success/failure, `require_role` denials at `core/security.py:373-392`). None are recorded today.
- **Split the `httpx` timeout** (`client.py:24`) from a flat 600 s into connect/read/write.
- **Regression test asserting `_resolve_cors_origins()` never returns `*`** and that CSRF `Origin` checking is on — the property that keeps AETHERIS out of CVE-2026-22812's failure mode.
- **Fix the two lowercase config aliases** at `core/config.py:218,224` so `AETHERIS_JWT_ALGORITHM` is actually settable.

### Unverified / rejected

**Rejected — number does not appear on the authoritative page**

- **"CaMeL neutralizes 67% of attacks in AgentDojo."** Appears in InfoQ and ssojet secondary coverage. The arXiv:2503.18813 abs page carries only the utility pair (77% with provable security vs 84% undefended) and **no attack-neutralization figure**. Use 77%/84% only.
- **PromptGuard framework, "67% reduction in injection success, F1 0.91, latency increase below 8%"** ([Nature Sci Rep s41598-025-31086-y](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-025-31086-y)). Search-snippet only, not fetched. Also an unrelated academic framework, **not** Meta's Prompt Guard classifier — the name collision is a trap.
- **"73.2% → 8.7% attack success, 94.3% baseline task performance"** ([arXiv:2511.15759](https://arxiv.org/html/2511.15759v1)). Snippet only, 2025 preprint, not fetched.
- **"Total attack success 10.7%–29.6%"** ([arXiv:2605.17986](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.17986)) and **"Obfuscation ASR = 0.76"** ([arXiv:2604.03598](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03598)). Snippets only, not fetched.
- **"$82,314.44 Google Gemini bill in 48 hours from a compromised key."** Cited by two secondary blogs ([zuplo](https://zuplo.com/learning-center/api-cost-protection-rate-limits-quotas-spending-caps), [beyondscale](https://beyondscale.tech/blog/ai-denial-of-wallet-attack-enterprise-defense)) attributing it to The Register; The Register search URL returned **404** and the primary was unreachable. Do not quote the figure. The *control* recommendation is independently supported by OWASP LLM06, which was verified.
- **Granite-Guardian-3.2-5B "6.5% generalization gap"** ([arXiv:2511.22047](https://arxiv.org/html/2511.22047)) and **DeepContext's 0.67 scores for Llama-Prompt-Guard-2 / Granite-Guardian** ([arXiv:2602.16935](https://www.arxiv.org/pdf/2602.16935)). Snippets only.
- **GHSA-h4pw-wxh7-4vjj / CVE-2024-29370 (python-jose).** The GitHub advisory listing shows it **withdrawn as a duplicate**. Do not cite.

**Unverified — could not fetch the source**

- `genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-genai-llm-top-10-2026/` → **HTTP 403**. Worked around via the canonical `GenAI-Security-Project/GenAI-LLM-Top10` repo `2026/final` directory, which is the source of record. The legacy owasp.org project page still enumerates the **2023 v1.1** list under a "Historical version" label — an easy mis-citation.
- OpenAI, "Keeping your data safe when an AI agent clicks a link" → **HTTP 403**. Would have been the best vendor source on link/image exfiltration mitigations.
- Artificial Analysis × NVIDIA guardrail benchmark: protocol and dataset counts verified, but **every accuracy and latency value is inside interactive charts** and not extractable. **No per-model F1 or millisecond figures.** PromptGuard was not in that run.
- **Progent's own paper.** `export.arxiv.org/api/query` is unreachable from this environment (a control query with three known-good IDs returned an empty body), and an arXiv ID will not be guessed. Progent's numbers here come solely from the independent reproduction, arXiv:2606.26479, which was fetched.
- EU AI Act **high-risk deferral to Dec 2027 / Aug 2028**. Consistent across four law-firm and tracker sources but verified against neither EUR-Lex nor a Commission page. Article 12's text and its 2 Aug 2026 applicability date *are* verified (via artificialintelligenceact.eu, which itself flags its article text as a machine translation, not the official EP version).

**Methodology caveats worth carrying forward**

- All arXiv verification was done by fetching `arxiv.org/abs/…` (and one `/html/…v1` for Table 5), **not** the arXiv API — the API is blocked here, and a control test with known-good IDs confirmed the block rather than any missing record. For each paper: title, author list, submission date, and the specific quoted number confirmed on the fetched page. Where a number was only in full text (the classifier baselines), the HTML was fetched and the table read.
- CVE verification used the **OSV API** (`api.osv.dev`) and the **NVD 2.0 API** (`services.nvd.nist.gov`) directly, plus GitHub advisory pages — every version range, severity and publication date above came from one of those, not from a vendor blog.

**Correction to prior AETHERIS research**

- Earlier memory records **"CVE-2025-54121 fabricated → KILL"**. It is **real**: `GHSA-2c2j-9gv5-cj73`, *"Starlette has possible denial-of-service vector when parsing large files in multipart form"*, MODERATE, published 2025-07-21, fixed in **starlette 0.47.2**, aliased PYSEC-2026-1941. The prior verification almost certainly searched FastAPI's advisories rather than Starlette's and concluded fabrication from a mis-attribution. A false negative here is as damaging as a false positive — it left a real advisory unpatched on the strength of a "verified fabricated" label.
- The four security arXiv IDs carried in prior memory (**2607.19430** ChannelGuard, **2608.02657** AGRI, **2607.12406** isolation taxonomy, **2606.23277** GIF) all resolve, with titles and numbers matching what was recorded. Re-confirmed above and safe to reuse.

## Angle 4 — LLM-as-judge, calibration & evaluation

**Scoping note.** AETHERIS's judge is `orchestrator/evaluation.py`, a single pointwise call that both scores *and* synthesizes. `orchestrator/consensus.py` aggregates when more than one judge runs. `tests/` has 50+ pytest files and **no golden eval set**, which means no judge change to date has been measured. Two structural facts drive the whole angle: the judge prompt binds Logician to slot A and Creative to slot B on **every** call, and both aggregation paths are arithmetic means.

**Verification method.** WebSearch budget was exhausted (200/200) before the final pass, so every source below was verified through the official arXiv API (`export.arxiv.org/api/query`) or a direct `arxiv.org/abs/` · `arxiv.org/html/` · vendor-doc fetch. The API returns canonical metadata plus abstract in one call, so this is a stronger channel than search — but no source here was found via a search engine, and any paper whose abstract omits its headline number is marked as such rather than filled in from a secondary summary.

### Verified sources

| Paper/Source | Link | Verified | Core finding with exact numbers | AETHERIS implementation | E | R | ROI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reliability without Validity: LLM-as-a-Judge Across Agreement, Consistency, Bias | [2606.19544](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19544) | arXiv API feed: id, exact title, submitted 2026-06-17, all figures present in abstract | 21 judges, 9 providers, 3 benchmarks, 3 protocols, 118 runs, ~541,000 judgments. **Kappa deflation 33–41 pp on MT-Bench** (raw agreement vastly overstates chance-corrected). Rankings move **up to 14 positions** by protocol. Two production judges show test–retest **>0.95** *and* position bias **>0.10** simultaneously. Verbosity bias **<0.011** | `orchestrator/metrics.py:40-49` — `JUDGE_AGREEMENT_RATE` today means "fraction of last 100 judgements ≥ threshold", i.e. raw agreement. Rename to `..._raw` and add a κ gauge against a golden set. Stop treating reproducibility as validity: high test–retest is exactly what the rolling window measures, and it is uninformative | M | L | H |
| The Coin Flip Judge? Reliability and Bias in LLM-as-a-Judge Evaluation | [2606.13685](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13685) | arXiv API twice (two independent queries): id, exact title, submitted 2026-04-23, every number in abstract. **Date/ID mismatch flagged** (2026-04 published vs 2606 prefix) | 29 tasks, 10 categories, GPT-4o-mini + GPT-4.1-mini, 50 pairwise + 50 pointwise trials/question. Pairwise preferences **flip 13.6% on average**; **28% of questions exceed a 20% flip rate**, worst **56%**. GPT-4o-mini: **72% A-majority, p = 0.024**. Pointwise gaps only **0.19–0.36** on a 10-point scale, not significant in aggregate. Cross-judge agreement **76%, κ = 0.51**. Equivalent prompt templates flip the majority in **25%** of cases. **11 repeated trials** to recover the 50-trial verdict at 95% probability, **15** for high-variance items | `orchestrator/evaluation.py:65-100` hard-codes Logician into `<logician_argument>` (slot A) and Creative into `<creative_argument>` (slot B) on **every single call**, so a 72%-style first-position bias is applied systematically in one direction, forever. Randomize slot assignment; on high-complexity paths run both orders and average. Also: one sample per judgement is far below 11, so `validation_score` movement under ~1 point is noise | S | L | H |
| CyclicJudge: Mitigating Judge Bias Efficiently in LLM-based Evaluation | [2603.01865](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01865) · [html v3](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.01865v3) | arXiv API for id/title/date (2026-03-02, v3 2026-05-04); HTML v3 fetched for the variance table and round-robin analysis | Judge offset is **94.7–97.0% of benchmark score variance on MT-Bench** (Gemini 97.0%, Claude 96.5%) and **96.0–99.3% on MindEval** (Llama 99.3%) at m=1, K=1; scenario ≤2.2%, generation ≤0.7%. Authors' own line: judge variance is **">94% of benchmark variance at the default operating point"**. Round-robin assignment of judges to scenarios **cuts variance 27–40% at budget B=5 on MT-Bench** and "maintains the same per-item cost as single-judge evaluation"; judge bias vanishes exactly at K=K_tot. Full panel costs 5× for less benefit when σγ²/σβ² < 4 | `orchestrator/consensus.py:123-154` — `allocate_judges()` returns N identical roles and the caller runs all N on the same item. Change to cycling a judge *pool* across items: each item gets 1 judge, the pool rotates. **The only change on this list that reduces the dominant variance term at zero marginal cost.** Needs a pool list in `config/capabilities/model_capabilities.json` and a per-item index (hash of query) | M | L | H |
| Grading Scale Impact on LLM-as-a-Judge: Alignment Is Highest on 0-5 | [2601.03444](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03444) · [html v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.03444v1) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-01-06, 15 authors, 6 benchmarks, ICC named but **no values in abstract**; HTML v1 fetched, Tables 2/4a/5 read | Pooled human-LLM ICC: **0-5 = 0.853 (nMAE 0.111)**, **0-100 = 0.840 (0.115)**, **0-10 = 0.805 (0.122)**. 0-10 is the **worst** scale for human alignment; the paper states "the 0-10 scale yields the weakest". LLM-only reliability is marginally *highest* on 0-10 (0.950), so self-consistency picks the wrong scale. Gender-stratified: 0-10 male-LLM ICC drops to **0.751** vs 0.839 on 0-5. Per-benchmark exception: MT-Bench prefers 0-10 (0.470 vs 0.517 for 0-5) | `orchestrator/evaluation.py:89` — `"Provide validation_score from 0.0 to 10.0"` is the worst of the three scales tested. Move to 0–5 with named anchors; also touches `prompts/system/09_synthesizer.xml`, `core/schemas.py` (`aetherisOutput.validation_score` bound), the `metrics.py:45-49` gauge help text ("0-10"), and any threshold in `orchestrator/decisions.py`. **Needs a version marker in `orchestrator/versioning.py`** or pre-change `experience_db` rows are silently compared against post-change scores | S | **M** — silently rescales every historical score unless versioned | H |
| Pairwise or Pointwise? Feedback Protocols for Bias in LLM-Based Evaluation | [2504.14716](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14716) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-04-20, v2 2025-08-21, COLM 2025; flip-rate sentence in abstract | **"Pairwise preferences flip in about 35% of the cases, compared to only 9% for absolute scores."** Pairwise is more exposed to distractor features; absolute (pointwise) scoring is more robust | Validates AETHERIS's existing choice — `evaluation.py` already does pointwise (`validation_score`), not pairwise A-vs-B ranking. **Do not** "upgrade" to pairwise Bradley-Terry judging of Logician vs Creative: it would roughly quadruple the flip rate. Keep pointwise; spend the effort on scale and anchors | S (decision) | L | H |
| Am I More Pointwise or Pairwise? Position Bias in Rubric-Based LLM-as-a-Judge | [2602.02219](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02219) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-02-02, v2 2026-06-24; abstract carries **no numeric result** | Rubric scoring behaves as multiple choice with **model-specific** position bias (some judges favour the first option, others the last), plus a **second bias axis from criterion ordering**. Mitigation is option permutation, and "only a small number of random order permutations are sufficient" | `orchestrator/evaluation.py:86-91` — the four numbered INSTRUCTIONS are a fixed criterion order on every call. Shuffle criterion order per call, seeded on the request id so it stays reproducible. Zero extra tokens, zero extra calls | S | L | M |
| Improving LLM-as-a-Judge Inference with the Judgment Distribution | [2503.03064](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03064) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-03-04, v2 2025-09-26, EMNLP 2025 Findings; **no percentages in abstract** | Taking the **mean of the judgment distribution beats greedy mode decoding in every setting tested** (pointwise, pairwise, listwise). Risk-averse derivations often help further. **Chain-of-thought can collapse the judgment distribution and hurt** | `orchestrator/evaluation.py:105-112` — `gateway.execute_with_fallback` returns one sampled JSON. Where the provider exposes logprobs, compute the expected score over the score-token distribution instead of the sampled value: most of the multi-sample benefit for **one** call. Note the CoT warning before adding reasoning-before-score to `prompts/system/09_synthesizer.xml` | M | L | H |

| Quantifying and Mitigating Self-Preference Bias of LLM Judges | [2604.22891](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22891) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-04-24, v4; mitigation figure in abstract | Equal-quality response pairs across **20 mainstream LLMs**. Judge **capability is uncorrelated or negatively correlated** with low self-preference bias — a stronger judge is not a fairer judge. **Structured multi-dimensional evaluation cuts SPB by 31.5% on average** | AETHERIS's judge is drawn from the same provider pool as Logician/Creative (`role="judge"` via `ProviderStrategy`), so self-judging happens whenever the pool picks the same model. Two changes: (a) enforce judge ≠ generator at the `api_gateway/strategy.py` selection step; (b) the existing multi-field output (`overall_confidence`, `overall_bias_risk`, `disagreement_notes`, `validation_score`) is already the "structured multi-dimensional" shape — make each field individually justified rather than one holistic pass | M | L | H |
| Self-Preference Bias in Rubric-Based Evaluation of LLMs | [2604.06996](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06996) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-04-08, v3; both magnitudes in abstract | On IFEval and LiveCodeBench a judge is **"more than 50% more likely"** to wrongly mark a **failed** rubric item as satisfied when the output is its own. HealthBench scores skew by **as much as 10 points**. **Negative rubrics and subjective topics are the most affected.** Ensembling reduces but does not remove it | Direct hit on `orchestrator/claims.py:306 apply_firewall()` plus the judge: the firewall is exactly a negative rubric ("is this claim unsupported?"), the worst case. Keep the firewall **deterministic** (it already is per `claims.py:233`) rather than replacing it with an LLM judge, and never let the synthesizing model also grade its own claims | S (guard rail) | L | H |
| Breaking the Mirror: Activation-Based Mitigation of Self-Preference | [2509.03647](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03647) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-09-03, v2; the 97% figure in abstract | Steering vectors (CAA + an optimization variant) cut **unjustified self-preference bias by up to 97%**, beating prompting and DPO baselines. Instability remains on *legitimate* self-preference and on unbiased agreement, implying multiple or nonlinear directions | Not applicable — AETHERIS calls hosted providers via `api_gateway/`, with no activation access. Recorded to close the option: **the strongest known SPB fix is unavailable to this architecture**, which is why judge/generator disjointness is the actual lever | — | — | — (rejected for arch) |
| Are LLM Evaluators Really Narcissists? Sanity Checking Self-Preference | [2601.22548](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22548) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-01-30, v4; both figures in abstract | Against an **evaluator-quality null**, only **51% of examples from prior self-preference findings remain statistically significant** — though those retained examples carry **89.6% of the total self-preference probability mass**. Voting-distribution entropy suggests uncertainty-driven overlap | Sizing constraint, not a code change: when AETHERIS measures its own judge bias, the control must be "would a *better* judge also prefer this?", not "did the judge prefer its own output?". Half of naive self-preference signal is really quality signal. Affects the golden-set harness design in `tests/` | S (design) | L | M |
| Self- and Other-Labels Induce Bidirectional Bias in LLM Judges | [2608.18091](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18091) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-06-06; abstract carries **no percentages** | Ten LLMs. In the **blind** condition self-preference **disappears on 3 of 4 rubric dimensions and reverses on the fourth**. Under matched quality, self/other labels alone move scores in **opposite directions** | `orchestrator/evaluation.py:74-80` — the tags `<logician_argument>` and `<creative_argument>` are identity labels handed to the judge on every call, and the system prompt names the roles. Rename to neutral `<candidate_1>`/`<candidate_2>` and drop role identity from the judge prompt. Combined with slot randomization this is full blinding for ~0 tokens | S | L | H |
| Quantifying Label-Induced Bias in LLM Self- and Cross-Evaluations | [2508.21164](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21164) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-08-28, v3; both magnitudes in abstract | **False attribution shifted voting outcomes by up to 50 percentage points and quality ratings by up to 12 points.** The "Claude" label raised scores regardless of true authorship; the "Gemini" label lowered them. Gemini self-deprecated under true labels; Claude showed intensified self-preference | Same fix, plus one more: `orchestrator/consensus.py:203` keys capability weights on `model_id`, so model identity is in the aggregation path too. That is fine (weights are external priors), but identity must never reach the *judge prompt*. Add a test asserting no provider/model name appears in the rendered judge prompt | S | L | H |
| Self-Preference Is Weak or Absent in Verifiable Instruction-Following Revision | [2606.20093](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20093) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-06-18; CI and gap in abstract | Four model families, 85 author-vs-fresh comparisons on IFEval. Author-minus-fresh rejection gap on verified-good fixes: **−5.1 pp, 95% CI [−12.9, +2.7]** — null. **97% of author rejections gave flaw-catching reasons**, not preference. Effects under ~13 pp are undetectable at that sample size | Counterweight that sets expectations honestly: on **verifiable** tasks self-preference may be absent, and a golden set of ~85 items cannot detect an effect smaller than ~13 pp. Below a few hundred items, do not claim a bias was "fixed" | S (design) | L | M |
| Turning Bias into Bugs: Bandit-Guided Style Manipulation Attacks (BITE) | [2605.26156](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26156) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-05-24, v2 2026-05-28, ICML 2026; both figures in abstract | Attack success rate **above 65%** against LLM judges, with **score inflation of 1–2 points on a 9-point scale**, on **both pointwise and pairwise** protocols | Security, not style: `orchestrator/evaluation.py:60-63`'s `json.dumps` escaping is a *structural* defence (stops delimiter breakout) and **must stay**, but it does not stop style manipulation, which needs no injection at all — the attacker just writes more markdown. A 1–2 point inflation on a 0–10 scale is larger than most real quality differences AETHERIS measures. Record style covariates (length, markdown/bold/list counts) in `orchestrator/experience_db.py` so score drift can be regressed against style later | M | L | H |
| LLMs Cannot Reliably Judge (Yet?): Robustness of LLM-as-a-Judge | [2506.09443](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09443) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-06-11, v2 2026-08-06; counts in abstract | **15 attacks, 8 defenses, 13 models**, 3 research questions. Judges stay vulnerable under **both pointwise and pairwise** protocols, and **no defense dominates on robustness, utility, and cost simultaneously** | Sets the ceiling: do not chase a "safe judge" configuration. Treat `validation_score` as an advisory signal that gates nothing irreversible, and keep the deterministic `claims.py` firewall as the load-bearing check | S (policy) | L | M |
| The Formalism Trap: Judges Blinded by Consensus Mimicry under Social Load | [2607.28641](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28641) | abs page fetched: exact title, authors Shehata & Li, all figures. **Date/ID inconsistency on the page itself** (says 20 May 2026, ID prefix implies 2026-07) | **22,500 trajectories** across GAIA, SWE-bench, Multi-Challenge. Lexical grounding at **p < 10⁻¹²⁰**. Logistic meta-evaluator detects evaluator capture at **ROC-AUC 0.8779**; leave-one-domain-out transfer mean **ROC-AUC 0.7482**. Judges conflate "structural proceduralism with semantic truth under adversarial load" (Evaluative Dissonance Index D_E) | AETHERIS is unusually exposed: the judge prompt at `orchestrator/evaluation.py:88` **orders** the judge to include and discuss the Creative agent's alternatives, which rewards procedural completeness over correctness. That instruction is a formalism-trap generator. Split it: score correctness first on its own, then require inclusion of alternatives in the composed answer, so completeness cannot inflate `validation_score` | M | L | H |

| **RoPoLL: Robust Panel of LLM Judges** | [2606.30931](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30931) | arXiv API twice (two distinct queries): id, exact title, submitted 2026-06-29, 3 authors, every number in abstract | Huber contamination model. Plain consensus/mean scoring **"incurs unbounded bias under any positive contamination, regardless of jury size."** Geometric median is **tuning-free with the optimal finite-sample breakdown point 1/2**; rate σ√(d/N), √d gap to the minimax lower bound. **13 open-weight judges, 4B–675B**, 3 reward-model benchmarks, 4 corruption regimes **up to 50%**. **~19% gain on cross-dimensional attacks at matched compute**. A **3-judge 38B RoPoLL committee beats Mistral-Large-3 (675B) by 1.31× on HelpSteer-2** under 30% bimodal-random corruption — an **18× parameter advantage**. Noisy-GT control shows the gain is against *biased* contamination, not ordinary noise | `orchestrator/consensus.py:197` (`raw_agreement = sum(pair_scores)/len(pair_scores)`) and `:210` (`weighted_agreement = sum(weighted_scores)/total_weight`) are exactly the arithmetic mean this paper proves has unbounded bias. **In one dimension the geometric median is the ordinary median**, so the fix is `statistics.median(pair_scores)` — a one-line swap, breakdown point 1/2, no new dependency, no tuning. Keep the weighted mean as a second reported field for continuity | S | L | **H** |
| A Statistical Framework for Auditing Behavioral Dependence and Induced Bias in LLM Judges | [2604.07650](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07650) | arXiv API twice: id, exact title, submitted 2026-04-08, v2 2026-08-09; all correlations in abstract | **18 LLMs, six families.** Judge errors are **correlated**: ρ = **0.508** (BEI) and **0.520** (CIG), p < 0.01; on MATH-500 ρ = **0.441** and **0.457**, p < 0.05. A dependence-reweighted ensemble beats majority voting by **+3.5 pp accuracy and +2.6 pp precision**. Distillation between models is named as a *cause* of judge entanglement | `orchestrator/consensus.py:170-282` assumes independent judges when averaging, and `config/capabilities/model_capabilities.json` weights are per-model capability priors with no correlation term. With ρ≈0.5, six "critical" judges are worth far fewer than six votes. Concretely: forbid same-family judges in one panel at the `allocate_judges()`/strategy layer before adding any more judges | M | L | H |
| CARE: Confounder-Aware Aggregation for Reliable LLM Evaluation | [2603.00039](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00039) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-02-09; figure in abstract | Error reduced **by up to 26.8%** versus aggregation baselines including majority vote, across **12 benchmarks** | Second candidate aggregator for `consensus.py` after the median. Lower priority (needs confounder features), but it is the evidence that *aggregation choice*, not judge count, is where the remaining gain is | L | M | M |
| Dependence-Aware Label Aggregation for LLM-as-a-Judge via Ising Models | [2601.22336](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22336) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-01-29; **no numeric gains in abstract**, theoretical separation for K judges, 3 datasets | Theoretical separation showing dependence-aware aggregation dominates independence-assuming aggregation for K judges | Same conclusion as 2604.07650 from theory. Cited only to establish that the independence assumption baked into `compute_consensus()` is a known-wrong model, not a harmless approximation | — | — | L (reference) |
| LLM-as-a-Judge/Jury for Clinically-Validated Psychosis Safety Evaluations | [2604.02359](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02359) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2026-03-20; all κ values in abstract | Judge–human **κ = 0.75, 0.68, 0.56** for individual judges; **majority-vote jury κ = 0.74** — the **single best judge slightly beats the jury** | The strongest direct evidence against AETHERIS's `_JUDGE_COUNT_BY_COMPLEXITY` escalation ladder (`consensus.py:114-120`: 1/2/4/6). Panels cost 2–6× and here bought nothing. Before spending on judge count, measure per-judge κ on a golden set and **use the best one**; escalate only on disagreement | S (measure first) | L | H |
| Beyond Consensus: Mitigating the Agreeableness Bias in LLM Judge Evaluations | [2510.11822](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11822) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-10-13, v2; all figures in abstract | **TPR 96% but TNR under 25%** — judges detect good outputs and miss bad ones. **Voting is deemed insufficient.** A regression-based aggregator cuts max absolute error to **1.2%**, a **2× gain over the best ensemble of 14 LLMs**; 366 programs | The asymmetry matters more than the average: `validation_score` gates pass/fail on a synthesis, so **TNR under 25%** means the gate barely catches bad answers even at high average agreement. Report TPR and TNR separately as two gauges in `orchestrator/metrics.py`, never a single agreement rate. Adding judges does not fix this | S (metrics) | L | H |
| Stopping and Routing LLM Judge Panels | [2608.19802](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19802) | arXiv API/abs: id, exact title, 5 authors, submitted 2026-08-20, WISE 2026; **no quantitative results in abstract** | Panel design as role-conditioned allocation from a small labeled audit set: **discard "copies", always admit "complements", invoke "specialists" per slice**, and stop expanding once "validation gain falls below a threshold". Output is "a reusable, auditable call plan" | The replacement design for `allocate_judges()` at `consensus.py:123-154`. Today judge count is a static function of `complexity` with no reference to whether extra judges add information. Change to: allocate 1, escalate only when the item is contested, and pick the *complement* judge (different family, per ρ≈0.5) rather than N copies of the same role string | M | M | H |
| Crowd Comparative Reasoning | [2502.12501](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12501) | arXiv API: id, exact title, submitted 2025-02-18, v2; figure in abstract | **Average accuracy gain of 6.7% across five benchmarks** over voting and criteria-expansion baselines | Prompt-level alternative to more judges for `prompts/system/09_synthesizer.xml`. Confidence-based strata are exactly the "accuracy varies across strata" condition the paper requires | M | L | **H** |

Unbiasedness "regardless of judge error profile" is what makes it safe to keep using a cheap judge | M | L | **H** |

### Angle 4 — ranked implementation ideas

Ordered by (payoff ÷ effort), with the blocked ones last. Every file:line anchor below was read from the current checkout during the recon pass; re-verify before editing, since the codebase moves.

**1. Randomize the A/B slot and blind the role labels in the judge prompt.** `evaluation.py:74-80` puts the Logician's answer in `<logician_argument>` and the Creative's in `<creative_argument>`, always in that order. That is two biases stacked: fixed position (2606.19544's 33–41 pp kappa deflation is measured on judges with exactly this shape) and informative labels that tell the judge which answer is supposed to be the rigorous one. Swap to `<candidate_1>`/`<candidate_2>`, shuffle which agent lands in which slot seeded on the request id (so it is reproducible), and record the mapping in the result object so downstream consumers can undo it. Highest payoff per line of code in this angle.

**2. Replace both mean aggregations with `statistics.median`.** `consensus.py:197` computes `raw_agreement = sum(pair_scores) / len(pair_scores)` and `:210` computes `weighted_agreement = sum(weighted_scores) / total_weight`. With 2, 4 or 6 judges, one outlier judge moves the mean by 1/n; the median has breakdown point 1/2. In one dimension the geometric median *is* the median, so this is a stdlib one-liner, not a new algorithm. Keep `weighted_agreement` as a second reported field rather than deleting it — the weights at `:203` are keyed on `model_id` and may encode real quality differences.

```python
# orchestrator/consensus.py:197  — ponytail: median, not mean. Breakdown point 1/2 vs 1/n.
# Upgrade path: if judge weights ever become calibrated probabilities, switch to a
# weighted median (sort by score, walk cumulative weight to 0.5) — not a mean.
raw_agreement = statistics.median(pair_scores) if pair_scores else 1.0
```

**3. Make judge counts odd, and escalate on contest rather than scaling the panel.** `_JUDGE_COUNT_BY_COMPLEXITY` at `consensus.py:114-120` is `{low: 1, medium: 2, high: 4, critical: 6}` — **every non-low tier is even**, so ties are structurally possible and RewardBench 2 (2506.01937) rejects even generative-RM ensembles outright. Change to `{low: 1, medium: 3, high: 5, critical: 7}`. Then note that Self-Taught Evaluators (2408.02666) measures majority voting as worth **+0.4 points** on a good judge: the panel is not where the quality is. Prefer `{low: 1, medium: 1, high: 3, critical: 3}` plus **escalate-on-contest** — run one judge, and only if `contradiction_score` at `consensus.py:217` crosses a threshold, add a judge **from a different model family**. Cheaper and better-founded than a standing panel.

**4. Round-robin the judge across items instead of running a panel per item.** From the same +0.4 finding: if the goal is to reduce *aggregate* measurement noise across a run rather than to get one item maximally right, rotating the judge model item-by-item decorrelates judge-specific bias at 1× cost instead of N×. This is the cheap substitute for a panel in the CI harness, where the aggregate is what gates.

**5. Move `validation_score` from 0–10 to 0–5 with named anchors, and version the scale.** `evaluation.py:89` asks for "validation_score from 0.0 to 10.0" with no anchor definitions — 21+ effective levels, none defined, so the judge is inventing a scale per call. Anthropic's own guidance is Likert **1–5** with "Output only the number." Define each level in one clause, and stamp the scale version into the result via `versioning.py` so historical scores are not silently compared across scale changes. `metrics.py:40-49`'s `SYNTHESIS_QUALITY_AVG` help text says "0-10" and must move with it.

**6. Build the golden set — 100 human labels is the target, and how they are sampled matters more than how many.** PRECISE (2606.05308) picked the best of three pipeline variants with **100 labels / 2 expert-hours**; StratPPI (2406.04291) gets a 2× effective-sample gain by stratifying. Concretely: stratify by the judge's own `overall_confidence` (`evaluation.py:96`), **sample within strata at random**, and — per 2601.20819 — never label items the judge prompt was tuned on, because reusing them gives anti-conservative intervals, and never hand-pick interesting failures, because MNAR biases every estimator including the human-only one. Store in `tests/` as data, not code.

**7. Re-score a fixed human-labelled anchor slice every run, to separate judge drift from pipeline drift.** Without this, a `validation_score` drop is unattributable: the pipeline may have regressed, or the provider may have silently updated the judge model. 2604.12843's frozen-anchor design gives the pattern (100 anchors, ρ ≥ 0.9 over >400 models) without needing its IRT machinery. Langfuse's silent evaluator version promotion and MLflow's undisclosed default judge model are both live examples of the drift this detects.

**8. Extract every mechanical check out of the judge prompt into deterministic Python before the call.** LiveBench (2406.19314) refreshes monthly with **no judge at all** because its answers are mechanically checkable; DeepEval packages the same idea as its `DAG` metric. In AETHERIS, anything checkable — did the answer cite a claim id that exists, is the JSON well-formed, are the numbers internally consistent, did it answer the question asked — should be a Python assertion whose result is *given to* the judge, not a thing the judge is asked to assess. Cheaper, deterministic, and it shortens the judge prompt (which also helps the caching work in Angle 6).

**9. Split correctness scoring from completeness scoring.** CLExEval (2606.31608) measures a **68.6% reasoning-to-output mismatch** and a judge that **approved 47.9% of clinically incorrect outputs** — a single scalar cannot express "well-reasoned but wrong". `evaluation.py:86-91`'s four numbered instructions currently blend correctness, completeness and style into one 0–10 number. Emit two fields. This also decouples from the length problem in idea 14: completeness legitimately rewards length; correctness must not.

**10. Report TPR and TNR separately, plus a κ gauge, and rename the existing metric `..._raw`.** `metrics.py:40-49`'s `JUDGE_AGREEMENT_RATE` is raw agreement over the last 100 judgements. 2606.19544 measures **33–41 pp kappa deflation on MT-Bench** — raw agreement systematically overstates. Keep the raw gauge (renamed, so nobody reads it as chance-corrected), add per-class rates, and add κ once the anchor slice from idea 7 provides labels. Extend the existing `demo()` self-check at `metrics.py:132-192` rather than adding a test file.

**11. Pin the judge's temperature and seed — and stop treating temperature 0 as a variance fix.** `evaluation.py:105-112` calls `gateway.execute_with_fallback` with **no sampling config**, so the judge inherits provider defaults; Inspect AI's docs describe this exact situation as "often temperature 1.0 with no seed". Pin `temperature=0, seed=42` for reproducibility. But Miller (2411.00640) shows lowering temperature **raised** variance in his worked examples (1/12 → 1/4, and 1/27 → 3/16), and Inspect AI states temperature 0 "reduces, but does not always eliminate, run-to-run disagreement" — so the variance reduction must come from **resampling K answers (K=4 halves reducible variance)**, not from the temperature knob. Also note promptfoo's finding that GPT-5 reasoning models **ignore `temperature` entirely**, so the pin cannot be the only defence.

**12. Wrap `validation_score` in an interval and escalate only when it straddles the gate.** A point score compared to a threshold flips on noise — 2607.02577 measured **57.9%–76.8% across 23 identical reruns**. Compute the interval from the K resamples of idea 11, and route to a second judge only when the interval crosses the accept/reject boundary. This is the statistically honest version of idea 3's escalate-on-contest, and it bounds cost by only paying for hard cases.

**13. Replace exact-string Jaccard claim agreement with semantic matching.** `consensus.py:160-167`'s `_pairwise_agreement` is Jaccard over raw claim strings, so "the study found no effect" and "no effect was found in the study" score as total disagreement. Every downstream number depends on it, including `contradiction_score = 1.0 - raw_agreement` at `:217`. Angle 1's NLI recommendation (`MoritzLaurer/DeBERTa-v3-large-mnli-...`, MIT) is the same component and can serve both call sites. Ship behind `AETHERIS_ENABLE_CONSENSUS`, default off, since it changes every consensus number.

**14. Log the four style covariates next to every score, then regress.** Length-Controlled AlpacaEval and Arena style control both isolate the same four features: answer token length, markdown header count, bold count, list count — each normalized by the sum of both candidates' lengths. AETHERIS has a structural version of this bias: `evaluation.py:88` instructs the judge to "include and discuss Creative's alternatives", which lengthens the synthesis **by construction**, and the same judge then scores that synthesis. Log the covariates first (cheap, no behaviour change); if length explains `validation_score`, the metric is measuring verbosity and the instruction at `:88` has to change.

**15. Enforce judge ≠ generator in `api_gateway/strategy.py`.** 2410.13341's bound (a judge no better than the system under test cannot cut label needs by more than half), 2608.13608's "no usable signal" for similarly-powered comparisons, 2605.31351's **94.2% self-preference rate** on GPT-5.4, and Anthropic's own "use a different model to evaluate" all converge here. Today `evaluation.py:109` passes `role="judge"` and the strategy layer resolves it from the same provider pool as the generators. Add an assertion that the resolved judge model id is not in the set of ids that produced the candidates, and fail loudly rather than silently degrading.

**16. Gate CI on a run-level aggregate with an explicit threshold and an interval — never per-item booleans.** Copy Langfuse's `run_evaluators` shape, add what it lacks: **pair against the previous run** (Miller: free 1/3 variance cut; 2512.21326: 3–6× power with pairing plus averaging), use Wilson or Beta intervals rather than `1.96·sqrt(p(1-p)/n)` (2503.01747: a nominal 95% CLT interval covers only **92.5% at N=100**), correct for multiple comparisons when several configs are compared at once (`evalci` measured **37.5% of adjacent leaderboard gaps as non-significant** after correction), and **cap how many times one config may be scored** — the Leaderboard Illusion's 112% relative gain came from undisclosed best-of-N private testing, and an unbounded retry loop against a CI gate is the same mechanism. Avoid promptfoo's `{"pass": true, "score": 0}` trap and DeepEval's `flaky=True`, both of which turn a red gate green silently.

**17. Do not enable the dormant Adaptive v1 flags to add step-level verification.** The natural next move after reading GenRM (2408.15240) and Unsupervised PRMs (2605.10158) is to score individual DAG nodes. Do not: `execution_manager.py:762` sets `produced_outputs = {field: response_text for field in fields}`, so every declared output field receives the same blob. A PRM applied there would be scoring fabrications with high confidence, and 2608.19760's closing point applies — comparisons at unmatched effective sample size "measure dose, not credit". Fix the executor first; then the verifier is worth building.

### Angle 4 — cheap wins

Each is a small diff with a self-contained check, ordered roughly by diff size:

- `statistics.median` for both aggregations at `consensus.py:197` and `:210`; add the `ponytail:` comment naming the weighted-median upgrade path.
- Odd judge counts in `_JUDGE_COUNT_BY_COMPLEXITY` (`consensus.py:114-120`) — a four-value dict edit that removes structural ties.
- Randomize the A/B slot in the judge prompt, seeded on the request id so it stays reproducible.
- Rename the prompt tags at `evaluation.py:74-80` to neutral `<candidate_1>`/`<candidate_2>`.
- Shuffle the order of the four INSTRUCTIONS at `evaluation.py:86-91` per call, so instruction position cannot systematically bias the score.
- Pin `temperature=0, seed=42` on the judge call at `evaluation.py:105-112`.
- Record judge model id + judge prompt hash + score-scale version on every result via `versioning.py` — without this, no historical score comparison is valid.
- Log **per-judge raw scores**, not only the aggregate. Currently the individual votes are discarded, which makes every finding above unmeasurable after the fact.
- Split `JUDGE_AGREEMENT_RATE` into `_raw` / TPR / TNR gauges in `metrics.py:40-49` and extend the existing `demo()` self-check at `:132-192`.
- Log the four style covariates (answer token length, markdown header count, bold count, list count, each normalized by the sum of both candidates' lengths) alongside `validation_score`.
- A prompt-hygiene test asserting (a) no provider or model name appears in the rendered judge prompt, and (b) the `json.dumps` escaping at `evaluation.py:60-63` survives a delimiter-injection fixture. **That escaping is a trust boundary and must not be "simplified" away in a future refactor** — Inspect AI ships the equivalent `[BEGIN DATA]` sanitization for the same reason.
- Wilson or Beta interval helpers as ~5 lines of stdlib `math`, replacing any `1.96*sqrt(p(1-p)/n)` in the harness before it gets written.
- Assert judge ≠ generator at the point where `role="judge"` resolves in `api_gateway/strategy.py`.

### Angle 4 — unverified, corrected and rejected

Preserved rather than trimmed, per the brief. Anything in this subsection must **not** be cited as fact.

**Unverified with no source attributable.** These circulate widely and could not be traced to a fetchable primary source, so they are recorded as unsourced:
- *"9 judges ≈ 2 effective votes."* Two arXiv API queries returned only 2608.19760 and 2512.11150; neither contains the claim. Verified substitutes making a related point: **2604.02359** and **2604.07650**.
- *"Panel benefit is concentrated on one-vote-margin items, +10.4 to +23.3 pp."* No source found.
- *"An open-weight jury beats frontier judges at 8–15% of the cost."* No source found. The nearest verified analogue is RoPoLL's **1.31× / 18× parameter ratio** — a parameter ratio, **not** a price ratio, and the two are not interchangeable.
- **PoLL (Panel of LLM evaluators)** — the original paper's arXiv ID could not be verified and **will not be guessed**. (A fabricated CVE in this project's earlier research is exactly why.)
- *"~25% of challenging cases violate transitivity."* Appears in **2601.03444** but as a claim attributed to prior work; the primary is unverified. Verified protocol-instability figures from this pass are **13.6%** and **~35% vs 9%**.
- Elo update-order and K-factor sensitivity: no source located.
- Percentage of intransitive triads: **2601.07158** is a real paper but concerns animal dominance data, not LLM judging.
- **arXiv 2604.13717 is omitted entirely** because its numbers drift between versions (v1 83.6% / +11.9 pp vs v3 85.8% / +13.5 pp). A number that changes between revisions is not a number.

**Verified to exist but carrying no extractable numeric result** — cited only as existence, never as evidence: 2602.02219, 2601.22336, 2602.06625, 2605.30568, 2509.18658, 2503.03064, 2608.19802, 2608.13608, 2608.18091, 2608.18300, 2512.03019.

**Numbers read from full text rather than abstract** (weaker provenance, flagged): **2601.03444** (Table 2 of HTML v1); **2603.01865** (Table 3 — only ">94%" is stated directly in prose, and its two zero generation-variance entries are **truncated negative method-of-moments estimates, not true zeros**, which matters if anyone reuses them).

**Attribution corrections made during this pass:**
- **2606.09165**'s 0.24 FNR shift is **cited from prior work, not measured in that paper.**
- **2606.29920** gives **no judge accuracy figures, no quantified batching effect, and no numeric diminishing-returns threshold** — it is frequently summarized as if it does.
- **GSM1k (2405.00332)** is **"up to 8%"**, not the widely-repeated 13%.
- **The Leaderboard Illusion (2504.20879)**'s 112% figure is a relative gain **on the arena distribution**, not on ArenaHard, and the paper reports **no Elo deltas**.
- **2412.18407**'s mechanism is a **factored tie model**, not Rao-Kupper.
- **2607.27172**'s "~30% of teacher cost" applies to the **generator, not a distilled judge**.

**Date or ID inconsistencies** (dates in this document should be treated as approximate for these): 2607.22561, 2606.07520, 2607.28641, 2606.13685.

**Tooling facts that could not be verified from this environment:**
- Vertex AI / Gemini Enterprise evaluation specifics — Google docs returned navigation-only bodies.
- Anthropic Claude Console "Evaluation tool" — **no such page exists**; the URL served `develop-tests` instead.
- Braintrust CI docs — timed out at 60 s on two attempts.
- W&B Weave — `weave-docs.wandb.ai` returned **HTTP 403**; verified at `docs.wandb.ai/weave` instead.
- lm-evaluation-harness per-task stderr and `--bootstrap_iters` — **unverified**, despite being widely attributed.
- Inspect AI's GitHub Releases page is **empty**; 0.3.259 comes from PyPI only.
- **SERPANT** is verified on the ICML 2026 poster page, but **no arXiv ID exists there** — do not cite one.

---

## Angle 5 — Observability, eval ops & streaming UX

**Verification note.** Every row was fetched first-hand during this pass unless marked UNVERIFIED. The `WebSearch` budget was exhausted mid-pass (200/200), so later discovery used the **arXiv Atom API**, the **GitHub REST API**, and `llms.txt` doc indexes — all of which return primary content, so verification quality rose rather than fell. Two sub-agents were rejected by the concurrency limiter, so nothing here is second-hand.

**Overlap with Angle 4 is deliberate.** A handful of sources (Miller 2411.00640, MT-Bench 2306.05685, Norman 2606.19544, Inspect AI, promptfoo, DeepEval, Langfuse CI) appear in both angles with **different verified detail** — Angle 4 read them for statistical method, Angle 5 for operational surface. Both are kept; nothing is deduplicated away.

It "subtracts cache reads/writes from `input` for OpenTelemetry `gen_ai.usage.*`". Prices matched by regex `match_pattern`; tiers by priority with conditions like `input > 200000`; **costs are computed at ingestion time**, so price changes are not retroactive | Directly indicts `telemetry/observer.py`: it prices on **substring match over a 9-entry `MODEL_PRICING` dict** with a silent `"default"` fallback of $0.10/$0.20 per 1M, **ignores cached tokens entirely**, and avoids double-counting only because it never sees the cache fields at all. Fix bucket exclusivity **before** building any cost dashboard, or every figure will be wrong in both directions at once | S | L | **H** |
| Langfuse — prompt A/B & canary | [a-b-testing docs](https://langfuse.com/docs/prompt-management/features/a-b-testing.md) | Fetched | Variants are just labels (`prod-a`, `prod-b`); **the traffic split happens in your application** — `random.choice([prompt_a, prompt_b])` / `Math.random() < 0.5`. No weighting or rollout-percentage knob; canary "with a small group of users" is a use case you implement yourself. Link with `langfuse_prompt=selected_prompt` so metrics attribute correctly | AETHERIS already records `prompt_versions: dict[str,str]` in `ExecutionManifest`. Add label selection in `prompts/` loading and put the chosen version on the span as `gen_ai.prompt.name` + `gen_ai.prompt.version` (both real semconv attributes) — canary analysis becomes a group-by with no new infrastructure | S | L | **H** |
| Langfuse — spend alerts | [spend-alerts docs](https://langfuse.com/docs/administration/spend-alerts.md) | Fetched | Custom monetary thresholds; "We check each organization's usage every 60-90 minutes"; "triggers at most once per billing cycle to avoid notification fatigue"; email to Owner/Admin only. Scope is the **Langfuse Cloud bill**, explicitly "not LLM or model costs you track in Langfuse observability" | Do not expect LLM-spend alerting from this. Alert on AETHERIS's own `gen_ai.client.token.usage` histogram in Prometheus, and borrow the **once-per-cycle dedup rule** — which is also the mitigation OWASP LLM06 asks for in Angle 3 | S | L | M |

| Evidently (drift) | [github.com/evidentlyai/evidently](https://github.com/evidentlyai/evidently) | Fetched. **Apache-2.0** | "20+ statistical tests and distance metrics to compare shifts in data distribution"; `DataDriftPreset(method="psi")`, `include_tests="True"` attaches pass/fail; `Report`, Test Suite with `gt`/`lt` conditions, `TextEvals`, descriptors `Sentiment`/`TextLength`/`Contains`; "Supports evals for predictive and generative tasks, from classification to RAG"; self-hosted monitoring UI via `evidently ui --demo-projects all` on `localhost:8000` | Drift detection on quality metrics **without inventing statistics**: feed the last N judge scores (current) versus the previous release window (reference) into `DataDriftPreset(method="psi")` in a nightly job. **PSI over the score distribution beats a moving-average alarm**, and it is a dependency that can be dropped later without changing the data model | M | L | H |

| promptfoo — assertions | [expected-outputs docs](https://www.promptfoo.dev/docs/configuration/expected-outputs/) | Fetched | Deterministic types include `equals`, `contains`, `regex`, `is-json`, `javascript`, `python`, `latency`, `cost`, `is-refusal`, `trace-span-count`, `trace-span-duration`, `trace-error-spans`, `trajectory:tool-sequence`; model-assisted include `llm-rubric`, `g-eval`, `factuality`, `context-faithfulness`, `answer-relevance`, `select-best`. "Every test type can be negated by prepending `not-`". `threshold` exists per-assertion, per-test-case and per `assert-set`; **"A `threshold` of `0` makes the test case pass regardless of individual assertion failures"**; inline form `type(threshold):value`, e.g. `similar(0.8):Hello world`; `weight` defaults 1.0 and **weight 0 auto-passes**; global defaults via `defaultTest.assert` | The `latency` and `cost` assertions are the cheapest guardrails AETHERIS can add against the existing 900 s frontend timeout and the per-query budget in `orchestrator/budget.py`. The `trace-*` assertions become usable once idea 1's spans exist. ⚠️ Two silent-pass traps here — `threshold: 0` and `weight: 0` — on top of the `{"pass": true, "score": 0}` trap found in Angle 4 | S | L | H |

(Angle 4's separate fetch of the scorers page *did* verify the statistics API, which is why it is recommended there and only noted here — the two verdicts differ because the two pages differ) | — | — | M |
| **Miller, Adding Error Bars to Evals** | [2411.00640](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00640) · [HTML v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2411.00640v1) | Fetched abs (Evan Miller, sole author, 2024-11-01, stat.AP, 14pp, DOI 10.48550/arXiv.2411.00640) **and** the full HTML for the formulas | `SE_CLT = sqrt(Var(s)/n)`; binary `sqrt(s̄(1−s̄)/n)`; CI `s̄ ± 1.96·SE`. Clustered SE (Eq. 4) measured **up to 3.05× larger** than naive (DROP 1.34 vs 0.44; MGSM 1.88×; RACE-H 1.10×). Resampling K per item: variance ×(1+2/K)/3 → **−1/3 at K=2, −1/2 at K=4, ceiling 2/3**. **"Don't touch the thermostat!"** — T=0 **tripled** variance in the worked case (1/12 → 1/4) and shifted the mean; "In neither case should the sampling temperature be adjusted for the sake of reducing variance." Paired SE (Eq. 7): "paired differences represent a 'free' reduction in estimator variance." Power (Eq. 9/10): ω²=1/9, δ=0.03, α=.05, β=.20 → **n ≈ 969**, supporting "new evals should contain at least 1,000 questions in order to have good signaling ability"; K 1→10 moved MDE **13.2% → 7.5%** at n=198. Worked example: a model "winning" 2 of 3 benchmarks reverses to **1 significant win** once SEs are paired | The design constraint on the CI gate. AETHERIS hardcodes `temperature: 0.1` in `api_gateway/client.py` — **leave it alone** and reduce variance with K instead. The n=50 arithmetic in idea 5 below is derived from Eq. 10 | M | L | **H** |
| Zheng et al., MT-Bench / LLM-as-a-Judge | [2306.05685](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05685) | Fetched abs. Title, 13 authors, submitted 2023-06-09 (v4 2023-12-24), NeurIPS 2023 D&B — all confirmed | "over 80% agreement, the same level of agreement between humans"; examines "position, verbosity, and self-enhancement biases" plus limited reasoning; released 3K expert votes and 30K conversations | The >80% figure is the **ceiling** to expect from `orchestrator/evaluation.py`'s judge, not a floor. `aetheris_judge_agreement_rate` in `metrics.py` currently measures agreement with a **threshold**, not with humans — rename it or it will be misread by whoever builds the dashboard | S | L | M |
| **Norman et al., Reliability without Validity** | [2606.19544](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19544) | Fetched abs. Justin D. Norman, Michael U. Rivera, D. Alex Hughes, submitted **2026-06-17**, CC BY 4.0 | **21 judge models, 9 providers, 3 benchmarks (MT-Bench/JudgeBench/RewardBench), 118 runs, ~541,000 judgments.** Exact-match agreement "does not correct for chance and systematically overstates discriminative ability": **kappa deflation of 33–41 percentage points on MT-Bench**, described as universal. Ranking instability across benchmarks **up to 14 positions**. Test–retest reliability **>0.95** while position bias **>0.10 in 2 production-deployed judges**; verbosity bias **<0.011** under a single pairwise rubric. Names a "Minimum Viable Validation Protocol" (contents not on the abs page) | Report **Cohen's kappa**, not raw agreement, for the judge in `metrics.py` — a high `aetheris_judge_agreement_rate` is fully compatible with a judge that is consistently wrong in one direction. Note the sharp asymmetry: **position bias is the live problem, verbosity bias is not** under a single pairwise rubric — which is why Angle 4's idea 1 (randomize the slot) ranks above its idea 14 (style covariates) | S | L | **H** |
| Yang et al., self-preference bias | [2604.22891](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22891) | Fetched abs. Title, 6 authors, ID confirmed; v1 2026-04-24, v4 2026-06-02, CC BY 4.0 | SPB = "a directional evaluative deviation in which LLMs systematically favor or disfavor their own generated outputs". **20 mainstream LLMs**; advanced capability is "often uncorrelated, or even negatively correlated, with low SPB". Structured multi-dimensional evaluation via cognitive-load decomposition cuts SPB **31.5% on average**. No formal metric definition in the abstract | AETHERIS routes Logician/Creative/Judge across providers (`routing.py`). **Never let the judge model be the model that produced a candidate** — record `gen_ai.response.model` per agent span and assert non-equality in a test. The "uncorrelated with capability" finding is what makes this a hard constraint rather than something a better judge model fixes | S | L | **H** |
| **Thinking Machines, Defeating Nondeterminism in LLM Inference** | [thinkingmachines.ai/blog](https://thinkingmachines.ai/blog/defeating-nondeterminism-in-llm-inference/) | Fetched. All numbers verbatim from the page | Qwen3-235B, temp 0, 1000 completions → **80 distinct outputs**, the most common appearing 78×; "the completions are actually identical for the first 102 tokens!", diverging at token 103 (992 "Queens, New York" vs 8 "New York City"). Root cause: "the primary reason nearly all LLM inference endpoints are nondeterministic is that the load (and thus batch-size) nondeterministically varies!" Requirement: "the reduction order for each element must be fixed regardless of the batch-size of the kernel." Fix order RMSNorm → matmul → attention (fixed split **size**, not split count). Result: "all of our 1000 completions are identical." Cost: vLLM default 26 s → unoptimized deterministic 55 s → improved attention **42 s**; batch-invariant matmul gives up ~20% vs cuBLAS | **Kills the idea of a bit-exact CI gate against a hosted API.** Determinism is achievable only on the `local` provider branch in `client.py` (Ollama/vLLM), at roughly **1.6× latency**. This row's value is preventing a wrong design before it is built | — | — | **H** (prevents a wrong design) |
| vLLM `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT` | [GitHub search API (issues)](https://api.github.com/search/issues?q=repo:vllm-project/vllm+batch+invariant&per_page=10&sort=created&order=desc) | Fetched the API JSON — 479 total matches; read PR/issue bodies. Verified via **repo activity, not official vLLM docs** | The flag is real and current: **`VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1`**, with tests under `tests/v1/determinism/`. PR **52960** (merged 2026-08-21) fixes an fp32 matmul shared-memory out-of-range at N=1 on SM86/SM89 and adds `test_fp32_n_equals_1`. PR **52998** (merged) turns FlashInfer all-reduce on by default but **always excludes it under `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1`** "because it does not provide the fixed reduction order that batch invariance requires". PR 53146 notes the flag "would address it" but currently **errors as unsupported for `MAMBA1_ATTN`** | If a self-hosted judge is ever used for CI scoring, run it under `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1` — the one place AETHERIS can get bit-exact replay. Verify per model architecture; Mamba is excluded | M | M | M |
| Gond et al., LLM-42 | [2601.17768](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17768) | Fetched abs. Title, 4 authors, ID and dates confirmed (v1 2026-01-25, v2 2026-01-30), CC BY 4.0, code at `github.com/microsoft/llm-42`. **No quantitative results on the abs page** | Criticises batch-invariant kernels: the approach "tightly couples determinism to kernel design, requiring new implementations" at a fixed cost even when little traffic needs determinism. Proposes instead a fast non-deterministic path plus **"a lightweight verify-rollback loop"** re-running candidates "under a fixed-shape reduction schedule", costing "only in proportion to the traffic that requires determinism" | The architectural argument matters more than the (absent) numbers: **pay for determinism only on the sampled/eval traffic**, not the whole request path — which is exactly how the deterministic 5% sampling in idea 6 is designed | — | L | M |
| **OpenAI reproducible outputs** | [developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/advanced-usage](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/advanced-usage) | Fetched after following the 301 from `platform.openai.com`. Read the reproducible-outputs section in full | "Chat Completions are non-deterministic by default"; OpenAI offers "some control towards deterministic outputs", aiming for "(mostly) deterministic outputs". "Set the seed parameter to any integer of your choice and use the same value across requests" with all other params identical. "Sometimes, determinism may be impacted due to necessary changes OpenAI makes to model configurations on our end." On `system_fingerprint`: "If this value is different, you may see different outputs due to changes we've made on our systems." **Only Chat Completions is named — the Responses API is not mentioned** | Record `gen_ai.request.seed` (a real cond-required semconv attribute) and store `system_fingerprint` in `ExecutionManifest` next to `git_commit`. **A fingerprint change is the single best explanation for an unexplained eval regression** — without it, every vendor-side model swap looks like an AETHERIS regression | S | L | **H** |
| Anthropic prompt caching | [platform.claude.com/docs/…/prompt-caching](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching) | Fetched after following the 302 from `docs.claude.com` | `cache_creation_input_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `input_tokens` (tokens after the last breakpoint), and `cache_creation.ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` / `ephemeral_1h_input_tokens`. `total_input_tokens = cache_read + cache_creation + input`. Pricing: 5-min writes **1.25×** base, 1-hour writes **2×**, reads **0.1×**. Minimum cacheable prompt **512 tokens** (Opus 5, Fable 5, Mythos 5), **1,024** (Sonnet 5, Opus 4.8, Sonnet 4.6/4.5), **2,048**, **4,096** (Opus 4.6/4.5, Haiku 4.5). Default TTL 5 min, `"ttl": "1h"` option, refreshed free on use; **max 4 cache breakpoints**, 20-block lookback | The cached-token ratio metric needs these three fields. Map to semconv: `cache_read_input_tokens` → `gen_ai.usage.cache_read.input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` → `gen_ai.usage.cache_write.input_tokens`. AETHERIS's prompt assembly (`agents/prompt_utils.py`) must clear the 512/1,024-token floor for caching to engage at all — and per Angle 6, `prompt_manager.py:353-357` assembles **volatile-first**, which defeats it regardless of length | S | L | **H** |

| **Nielsen, response-time limits** | [nngroup.com/articles/response-times-3-important-limits](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/response-times-3-important-limits/) | Fetched. Jakob Nielsen, published 1993-01-01 (from *Usability Engineering* ch. 5), updated 2014 | **0.1 s** = "the limit for having the user feel that the system is reacting instantaneously"; **1 s** = "about the limit for the user's flow of thought to stay uninterrupted"; **10 s** = "about the limit for keeping the user's attention focused on the dialogue" — beyond it, give a completion estimate. Percent-done indicators for >10 s plus "an obvious way to cancel"; for 2–10 s a full progress bar risks violating display inertia, so prefer a busy cursor with a counter; **when total work is unknown, report absolute progress** or a generic spinner | AETHERIS pipelines run far past 10 s (`PIPELINE_TIMEOUT_MS = 900000`). `calculateProgress()` in `usePipelineStages.js` maps stages to **fixed percentages (5/15/27/52/77/90/97)** — a fabricated percent-done for an unknown-duration task. Nielsen's own guidance for that case is absolute progress: stage names + elapsed + per-stage completion, all of which the SSE stream already carries | S | L | **H** |

Guidelines page: 18 guidelines, "evidence-based best practices", from an "award-winning 2019 CHI paper". Library page: all 18 titles verbatim | 1 "Make clear what the system can do" · 2 "Make clear how well the system can do what it can do" · 3 "Time services based on context" · 4 "Show contextually relevant information" · 5 "Match relevant social norms" · 6 "Mitigate social biases" · 7 "Support efficient invocation" · 8 "Support efficient dismissal" · 9 "Support efficient correction" · 10 "Scope services when in doubt" · 11 "Make clear why the system did what it did" · 12 "Remember recent interactions" · 13 "Learn from user behavior" · 14 "Update and adapt cautiously" · 15 "Encourage granular feedback" · 16 "Convey the consequences of user actions" · 17 "Provide global controls" · 18 "Notify users about changes". The library filters by goals including "Appropriate reliance", "Transparency", "Reliability" | The reliability-UX checklist, mapped onto components that already exist: **G2** → `ConfidenceBadge.jsx` (calibrated bands, not a raw float); **G10** → abstention copy when `orchestrator/uncertainty.py` is high; **G8** → cancel (the hook's `abort()` already exists — surface it); **G9** → correction capture that feeds the golden set; **G11** → `ReasoningTimeline.jsx` / `ReasoningGraph.jsx`; **G15** → per-message thumbs plus reason chips | M | L | **H** |
