Terminology verified: the process is **"Critique Shadowing"** and the role is **Principal Domain Expert** (not "benevolent dictator", as often misquoted) | "My heuristic is that I start with around **30 examples** and keep going until I do not see any new failure modes"; the root-cause pass used **20 failed interactions** and "You can get quite far in just 15 minutes". Binary only at first — "Did the AI achieve the desired outcome?"; 1–5 dashboards rejected because "It's not actionable" and "these metrics do not matter". Each verdict is paired with a critique "detailed enough so that you can use it in a few-shot prompt for a LLM judge". Honeycomb case: "It took us only three iterations to achieve > 90% agreement", with the caveat that "using raw agreement is generally not recommended and can be misleading when classes are imbalanced" — prefer precision and recall separately. Cites Shankar et al. on **"criteria drift"** | **The cheapest path to AETHERIS's first golden set:** 30 traces, one expert, binary verdict plus written critique, and the critiques become few-shot examples in the judge prompt under `prompts/`. Re-run the loop whenever `architecture_version` bumps — CI already gates that value. Note the independent agreement with Norman 2606.19544 on raw agreement, reached from practice rather than from statistics | M | L | **H** |
| **Hamel Husain, Your AI Product Needs Evals** | [hamel.dev/blog/posts/evals](https://hamel.dev/blog/posts/evals/) | Fetched | Three levels, "The cost of Level 3 > Level 2 > Level 1": "I often run Level 1 evals on every code change, Level 2 on a set cadence and Level 3 only after significant product changes." L1 = "Unit tests for LLMs are assertions (like you would write in pytest)", which must "run fast and cheaply"; "Rechat has hundreds of these unit tests"; **"you don't necessarily need a 100% pass rate. Your pass rate is a product decision."** Synthetic bootstrap: "I often utilize an LLM to generate these inputs synthetically", prompting for batches of **50**. "You must remove all friction from the process of looking at data"; judge alignment on batches of **25–50**; a custom review UI "in less than a day". Closing advice: **"Keep it simple. Don't buy fancy LLM tools. Synthetic generation gets the golden set to 50 items today without waiting for traffic — which matters because AETHERIS has no production traffic to sample | S | L | **H** |
| Villavicencio et al., uncertainty granularity | [2605.28571](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28571) (via arXiv Atom API) | Retrieved through the arXiv API feed; id, title, author and date read from the feed entry | Between-subjects, **N=192**, medical questions, uncertainty shown at **output, relation, and token level**. **Token-level displays raised agreement with the AI**; output- and relation-level did not, and instead **lowered users' confidence in their own answers**; relation-level cues **cut external verification** (searches, URL checks) | Direct warning for `ConfidenceBadge.jsx`: a **single top-level confidence number is the variant that reduced user self-confidence without improving outcomes**. Prefer claim-level marks — AETHERIS already has `orchestrator/claims.py` and a claim-extraction feature flag, so the better UI is already half-built | M | L | **H** |
| Gor et al., delegation & trust | [2605.28255](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28255) (arXiv API) | Retrieved via API feed | 24 matches, 23 expert humans × 16 AI agents → **387 delegation and 1,440 adoption decisions**. Humans missed **3.9%** of chances to follow correct AI advice and over-relied on misleading advice in **1.7%**; **under-reliance reached 64.5%** when the AI echoed a human's initial wrong answer; model confidence sat near chance on disagreements | **Under-reliance, not over-reliance, was the dominant failure.** Argues for surfacing the *disagreement between AETHERIS agents* (Logician vs Creative) rather than one blended confidence — `consensus.py` already computes it and the UI already has a `JudgePanel.jsx` to show it | S | L | M |
| Li et al., Learning to Trust | [2603.22634](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.22634) (arXiv API) | Retrieved via API feed | **N=200** across 4 calibration conditions (standard, overconfident, underconfident, reversed); accuracy, discrimination and calibration all improved significantly over **50 trials**; many participants failed in the reversed-mapping condition | Users recalibrate to whatever confidence scale ships, so **the scale must be stable across releases**. Version the confidence mapping and put it in `ExecutionManifest` alongside `prompt_versions` — the same versioning requirement Angle 4's idea 5 places on `validation_score` | S | L | M |
| Rosbach et al., automation bias | [2603.11821](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11821) (arXiv API) | Retrieved via API feed | **28** pathology experts; AI raised performance but produced a **7% automation-bias rate** where correct independent calls were reversed by wrong advice; time pressure did not change frequency but **worsened severity**; **higher in-task confidence predicted more dependence** | Quantifies the cost of a confident-but-wrong stream, and justifies abstention copy (HAX G10) over a hedged answer when `uncertainty.py` is above threshold. The "confidence predicts dependence" finding is the mechanism that makes `ConfidenceBadge.jsx` a safety surface, not decoration | S | L | M |
| **pytest-recording (VCR.py)** | [github.com/kiwicom/pytest-recording](https://github.com/kiwicom/pytest-recording) | Fetched. **MIT**; CPython 3.10–3.14 plus PyPy 7.3 | "A pytest plugin powered by VCR.py to record and replay HTTP traffic". `--record-mode=once` (values seen: `none` **default**, `once`, `all`, `rewrite`); "`pytest-recording` uses the `none` VCR recording mode by default to prevent unintentional network requests"; `--block-network` (plus `--allowed-hosts=httpbin.*,localhost`), `--disable-recording`; `@pytest.mark.vcr`, `@pytest.mark.default_cassette("example.yaml")`, `@pytest.mark.block_network`, a `vcr_config` fixture (e.g. `{"filter_headers": ["authorization"]}`), and a `vcr` fixture exposing `vcr.play_count`. Network blocking covers "`socket`-based transports and `pycurl`" | **The flakiness answer for the per-PR tier.** `api_gateway/client.py` uses `httpx`, so cassettes make the whole 4-stage pipeline hermetic, free and byte-stable — determinism by **recording**, not by seeds, which is the only kind available against hosted APIs. ⚠️ Set `filter_headers: ["authorization"]` so the provider API keys never land in a committed cassette — this is the same secret-exposure risk as the `.env` finding in the earlier audit | S | L | **H** |

### Angle 5 — ranked implementation ideas

**1. Emit OTel GenAI telemetry at the one existing choke point — highest ROI, smallest diff.** `api_gateway/client.py:122` already has `provider`, `actual_model`, `url`, `usage` and the response in scope, and already calls `observer.track_usage(...)`. Wrap that one block. **Do not instrument 40 orchestrator modules.**

```python
# telemetry/otel.py  — ponytail: one module, no abstraction layer
from opentelemetry import trace, metrics
_tracer = trace.get_tracer("aetheris")
_m = metrics.get_meter("aetheris")
TOKENS = _m.create_histogram("gen_ai.client.token.usage", unit="{token}")
DUR    = _m.create_histogram("gen_ai.client.operation.duration", unit="s")
```

Inference span (child of the agent span), name `chat {actual_model}`, kind CLIENT:

| Attribute | Value in AETHERIS | Level per spec |
|---|---|---|
| `gen_ai.operation.name` | `"chat"` | Required |
| `gen_ai.provider.name` | `groq` / `openai` / `mistral_ai` / `gcp.gemini` for those providers; `openrouter`, `nvidia`, `github`, `kie`, `unli`, `local` have **no listed enum value** — emit as-is (custom) | Required |
| `gen_ai.request.model` | `actual_model` | Cond. required |
| `gen_ai.output.type` | `"json"` (the payload sets `response_format={"type":"json_object"}`) | Cond. required |
| `gen_ai.request.stream` | only when streaming | Cond. required |
| `gen_ai.conversation.id` | the real conversation id — **never** `request_id`; the spec forbids UUID/trace-id fallbacks | Cond. required |
| `error.type` | exception class or HTTP status on the `httpx.HTTPStatusError` path | Cond. required, **Stable** |
| `gen_ai.request.temperature` | `0.1` (currently hardcoded) | Recommended |
| `gen_ai.response.model`, `.id`, `.finish_reasons` | from `data` | Recommended |
| `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` / `.output_tokens` | `usage["prompt_tokens"]` / `["completion_tokens"]` | Recommended |
| `gen_ai.usage.cache_read.input_tokens` / `.cache_write.input_tokens` | `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (OpenAI shape) / `cache_read_input_tokens` + `cache_creation_input_tokens` (Anthropic shape) — **currently dropped on the floor** | Recommended |
| `gen_ai.response.time_to_first_chunk` | streaming only | Recommended |
| `server.address` / `server.port` | host of the `url` already branched on / `443` | Recommended / Cond. required, **Stable** |
| `gen_ai.prompt.name` / `gen_ai.prompt.version` | from `ExecutionManifest.prompt_versions` — makes prompt canaries a group-by | Cond. required |

Content attributes (`gen_ai.input.messages`, `gen_ai.output.messages`, `gen_ai.system_instructions`) stay **off**: `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_GENAI_CAPTURE_MESSAGE_CONTENT=NO_CONTENT`, which is already the `opentelemetry-util-genai` default. If they are ever turned on, route through the Collector redaction processor with `blocked_values` mirroring `_EMAIL_RE` / `_LONG_DIGITS_RE` / `_PHONE_RE`. Note that `logs/model_io.log` (~6 MB of unrotated plaintext prompts and responses, per the recon pass) is the *existing* version of this exposure and should be fixed first.

**2. Span tree for the 4-stage pipeline.** Drive it from the transition hooks `state_machine.py` already supports (`on_transition`), so `pipelines.py` needs no restructuring. `PipelineState` already sequences `normalizing → breach_checking → generating → evaluating → synthesizing → formatting`:

```
invoke_workflow micro_mode                INTERNAL  gen_ai.operation.name=invoke_workflow
│                                                   gen_ai.workflow.name="micro_mode"   ← low cardinality, MUST
│                                                   gen_ai.conversation.id=<conversation.py id>
│                                                   aetheris.request_id / .git_commit / .architecture_version
│                                                   aetheris.prompt_fingerprint  (execution_replay.prompt_fingerprint)
├── invoke_agent Breaker                  INTERNAL  └── chat {model}          CLIENT
├── invoke_agent Logician                 INTERNAL  └── chat {model}          CLIENT   ┐ siblings,
├── invoke_agent Creative                 INTERNAL  └── chat {model}          CLIENT   ┘ concurrent
├── retrieval {gen_ai.data_source.id}     CLIENT    (knowledge_layer / retrieval.py; top_k → gen_ai.retrieval.top_k)
├── search_memory                         CLIENT    (memory_hierarchy.py; gen_ai.memory.store.id)
├── execute_tool {gen_ai.tool.name}       CLIENT    (skills.py / tools/)
└── invoke_agent Judge                    INTERNAL  └── chat {model}          CLIENT
        └── event gen_ai.evaluation.result  ×N      (parented to the judged span, per spec)
```

`plan {agent}` wraps `strategic_planner.py` / `execution_planner.py` when the planner runs, with its LLM call as a **child** (a spec requirement) and the resulting node spans as siblings under `invoke_agent`. The metrics then fall out for free — `gen_ai.invoke_workflow.duration`, `gen_ai.invoke_agent.duration`, `gen_ai.invoke_agent.inference_calls`, `gen_ai.invoke_agent.tool_calls`, `gen_ai.execute_tool.duration` — remembering that agent-level counts **exclude** sub-agent work.

Error attribution becomes a query instead of a log hunt: `error.type` on the deepest span names the stage that failed, the root workflow span carries the user-visible outcome, and `gen_ai.client.operation.exception` (severity WARN / 13) carries the provider detail.

**3. Dual-write the SSE stream and the trace from one call site.** `streaming.py` has 19 `EventType` values and `execution_replay.py` has 11 `ReplayEventType` values — **two parallel vocabularies, neither of which is the trace**. Add span events inside `StreamingManager.emit_event` (one line: `span.add_event(event.event.value, event.data)`) and put `trace_id`/`span_id` into the SSE payload. That single change makes every UI event clickable through to a trace, and turns `ExecutionTrace.trace_id` into a real W3C trace id instead of a private identifier — which is what makes incident forensics work at all.

**4. Judge scores as `gen_ai.evaluation.result`, and fix what `metrics.py` claims.** `orchestrator/metrics.py` exposes `aetheris_judge_agreement_rate` as "fraction of the last 100 judgements scoring at or above the agreement threshold" — that is a **threshold pass rate, not agreement**, and per Norman et al. exact-match-style agreement overstates discrimination by **33–41 pp** versus Cohen's kappa. Emit the standardized event, keep the Prometheus gauge as a mirror, **rename the metric to `aetheris_judge_threshold_pass_rate`**, and add a real `aetheris_judge_kappa_vs_human` once the annotation set exists. Also assert judge model ≠ candidate model.

**5. Minimum viable golden set plus a CI gate that is statistically honest.**

*Golden set (50 items, buildable this week):* 30 real traces triaged binary pass/fail by one Principal Domain Expert with written critiques (Hamel: start at 30, stop when no new failure modes appear) plus 20 synthetically generated inputs covering the branches `pipelines.py` already exercises — injection attempt, tool-required, retrieval-required, ambiguous/abstain, adversarial-long. Store as `tests/golden/set_v1.jsonl` with `{id, query, cluster, expect: {assertions[], rubric}, critique}`. **Note `cluster`:** Miller's clustered SE is up to **3.05×** the naive one, and synthetic variants of one seed prompt *are* a cluster — ignoring that turns a passing gate into a coin flip.

*The honest statistics at n=50* (derived from Miller Eq. 10; assumptions stated): paired binary scoring, per-model variance 0.16 (p≈0.8), inter-model correlation 0.5 → ω² = 2(0.16) − 2(0.5)(0.16) = 0.16. MDE at α=.05, β=.20 is `2.8·sqrt(0.16/50)` ≈ **15.8 pp**. A 50-item aggregate score gate can therefore only detect catastrophes. The consequences:

- **Tier 1 — per PR, hermetic, $0, hard fail.** Add to the existing `pytest -m "not slow"` job: `--block-network --record-mode=none` with VCR cassettes and `vcr_config = {"filter_headers": ["authorization"]}`. Assert only deterministic properties — JSON schema validity 100%, `parse_and_repair` never returns a fallback, no PII regex hit in any recorded output, the breaker blocks the injection fixtures, state-machine transitions legal, cost and latency budgets from `budget.py` respected. These are exact-match assertions on recorded bytes, so one failure is a real regression and **no statistics are needed**.
- **Tier 2 — nightly, live, paired.** Run the 50 items, judge with **K=3** samples and take the **median** (Miller: K=2 cuts variance 1/3, K=4 cuts it 1/2 — and do **not** touch temperature to buy variance). Compare **per item** against `tests/golden/baseline.json` from the last green run. Gate on **zero pass→fail flips** among items that passed the baseline (paired, exact, and statistically valid at n=50), with advisory reporting of the aggregate mean, its paired SE, and the cluster count. Fail the job on any flip; post the aggregate as a comment, **never** as a gate.
- **Growth path with real numbers:** ~300 items → MDE ≈ 6.5 pp; ~970 items → ≈ 3.5 pp, matching Miller's n≈969 for a 3 pp effect and the "at least 1,000 questions" guidance. Getting that many labels is what idea 7 is for.
- Report question count, cluster count, SE, and the paired difference with its SE in the job summary. **Anything less is a contest, not an experiment.**

**6. Online eval with deterministic sampling, reusing code that already exists.** Langfuse's design — 5% sampling, "deterministic per observation", $0.01–0.10 per assessment — is reproducible in ~15 lines: `int(prompt_fingerprint(query)[:8], 16) % 100 < 5`. Because `prompt_fingerprint()` normalizes whitespace and lowercases, **the same queries are sampled every release**, which is what makes week-over-week quality comparable at all. Judge those samples, emit `gen_ai.evaluation.result`, and once a night feed the score distribution (current window) versus the previous release window into Evidently's `DataDriftPreset(method="psi")` with `include_tests="True"`. A PSI test on the score distribution is a far better alert than a threshold on a moving average, and it is one dependency that can be dropped later without changing the data model.

**7. Feedback capture that is actually reusable as eval data.** HAX G9/G15 (efficient correction, granular feedback) plus Langfuse's keyboard-driven queue give the shape: per-message thumbs plus a reason chip plus an optional corrected output, written into the **same JSONL schema as the golden set** with `{trace_id, span_id, prompt_fingerprint, git_commit, architecture_version, verdict, critique}`. Because `ExecutionManifest` is already frozen per request, every label is permanently attributable to the exact code and prompt versions that produced it — which is what makes labels reusable for judge alignment and later fine-tuning instead of a pile of unattributable thumbs. Build it into `TelemetryDrawer.jsx` / `JudgePanel.jsx`; Hamel's own advice is that a custom review UI takes under a day and beats a generic one.

**8. Backend choice: emit vendor-neutral, decide later.** The investment is the OTLP emission; the backend is a URL. On the evidence gathered: **Phoenix** is the lightest fit today (one container, `PHOENIX_SQL_DATABASE_URL` pointed at the Postgres already running, OTLP HTTP on `:6006/v1/traces`, gRPC `:4317`) at the cost of ELv2 and a documented default admin password of `admin` under a 4-character policy — change both on first boot. **Langfuse** is the better product (MIT core per its handbook, native OTLP at `/api/public/otel/v1/traces`, evaluators with 5% deterministic sampling, annotation queues, prompt labels) at the cost of ClickHouse + Redis + S3 + Postgres all pinned to UTC, and no gRPC. **Opik** has the cleanest license story (Apache-2.0 for the whole platform) with first-class pytest and Online Evaluation Rules. **Skip OpenLLMetry** — it exists to auto-instrument SDKs, and AETHERIS calls providers with raw `httpx`.

**9. Deterministic replay, scoped to where it is achievable.** `execution_replay.py` already has three modes and a documented exit-gate property, but `replay_trace()` currently just re-emits recorded events for all three modes, so **"shadow" does not yet recompute anything**. Wire shadow mode to VCR cassettes for hosted providers — recording is the only determinism available there, given 80 distinct completions out of 1,000 at temperature 0 — and reserve bit-exactness for the `local` provider branch under `VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT=1` (~1.6× latency: 26 s → 42 s in the published benchmark; unsupported for `MAMBA1_ATTN`). Store `system_fingerprint` in the manifest: for hosted models it is the only signal that distinguishes "our change regressed quality" from "the vendor changed the backend".

**10. Cost/quality dashboard — the metric set worth tracking**, each derivable from the spans and events above with no extra plumbing: cost per accepted answer (cost ÷ verdicts labelled pass) · **cached-token ratio** (`gen_ai.usage.cache_read.input_tokens ÷ total input` — worth 10× on read price and 0.8× net on write, but only once prompts clear the 512/1,024-token floor) · task success rate (`gen_ai.evaluation.result` pass rate) · escalation/abstention rate · repair rate (`repair_started` events) · judge kappa versus human labels · tokens and duration p50/p95 from the two spec histograms · **time-to-first-chunk** (the number the user actually feels) · breaker pass rate and provider health (both already in `metrics.py`).

### Angle 5 — cheap wins (≤1 day each)

1. **Delete the fake telemetry.** `telemetry/observer.py:22-34` ships a 24-point `sparkline_history` seeded with invented values (42.0 … 76.0), synthesizes activity points as `(input+output)/50`, and with no data returns `avg_response_s = "1.2"` and `success_rate = "99.4"`. **A dashboard that reports 99.4% success before the first request is worse than a blank one.** Return `None`/empty and let the UI render an empty state — `EmptyState.jsx` already exists.
2. **Stop discarding cached-token counts.** `client.py:122` reads only `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens`. Add `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (OpenAI shape) and `cache_read_input_tokens`/`cache_creation_input_tokens` (Anthropic shape), and make the buckets non-overlapping (`input` excludes cached, per the exclusivity rule) — otherwise every cost figure is wrong in both directions at once.
3. **Price honestly.** `MODEL_PRICING` matches by substring over 9 entries with a silent `"default"` of $0.10/$0.20 per 1M. Add a `matched: bool` flag and count fallbacks in a metric, so unpriced models are **visible** rather than plausible.
4. **`--block-network` on the existing test job.** One flag on the current `pytest -m "not slow"` invocation proves the suite is hermetic today, before any cassettes exist.
5. **Move `temperature: 0.1` out of the hardcoded payload** into config, record it as `gen_ai.request.temperature`, and put it in `ExecutionManifest`. Miller's "Don't touch the thermostat!" only holds if you know where the thermostat is.
6. **Replace the fabricated progress bar.** `calculateProgress()` returns fixed percentages (5/15/27/52/77/90/97) for a task with a 900 s timeout. Nielsen's guidance for unknown-duration work is absolute progress: current stage name, completed stages, elapsed time — all already in the SSE payload — and an honest spinner.
7. **Surface cancel.** `usePipelineStages` already exposes `abort()`. Put a visible Stop control on any run past ~10 s (Nielsen: >10 s needs "an obvious way to cancel"), emit the existing `node_cancelled` replay event, and set `error.type` on the open spans so cancellations stop looking like failures.
8. **Ship forensics identifiers to the client.** Add `git_commit`, `architecture_version` and `prompt_fingerprint` (all already computed in `execution_manifest.py` / `execution_replay.py`) to the final SSE `result` event, so a screenshot of a bad answer is enough to find the trace.
9. **Rename `aetheris_judge_agreement_rate`.** It measures a threshold pass rate. Given the 33–41 pp kappa deflation finding, the current name **will** be read as human agreement by whoever builds the dashboard.
10. **Assert judge ≠ candidate model** in a 5-line test over `routing.py` output — the cheapest possible mitigation for self-preference bias.

### Angle 5 — unverified and rejected

**Unverified (promising, could not be confirmed this pass):**
- **Langfuse MIT license split.** The fetched `/self-hosting` page contains **no** license statement (only "Some add-on features require a license key" and (EE) tags). The "All product capabilities … are MIT licensed" wording came from a search snippet of `/handbook/chapters/open-source`, which was not fetched before the search budget ran out. **Treat MIT as unconfirmed**; check `LICENSE` in `langfuse/langfuse` before relying on it.
- **Langfuse support for the *new* content attributes.** The fetched mapping table lists `gen_ai.prompt` / `gen_ai.completion`; `gen_ai.input.messages` / `gen_ai.output.messages` do not appear. Absence from one page is not proof of non-support.
- **promptfoo CI gating on thresholds.** Neither fetched page documents an exit code or a fail-on-threshold flag; the only failure control shown is `|| true` plus a file-existence check. Do not design a gate around `promptfoo eval`'s exit status without verifying it.
- **`FastAPIInstrumentor.instrument_app(app)` and the excluded-URL env vars.** Not present on the PyPI page for `opentelemetry-instrumentation-fastapi` 0.65b0; the snippet is widely used but was not verified from a primary source.
- **Inspect AI's license and its `stderr()` metric** *from this angle's fetch*. The site links a LICENSE file but names no license, and the `stderr()` claim reached this angle only via Miller's paper. (Angle 4 subsequently verified `stderr()` from the Inspect scorers page directly — the license remains unverified.)
- **Phoenix `PHOENIX_COLLECTOR_ENDPOINT`, `PHOENIX_ENABLE_AUTH`, `PHOENIX_SECRET`.** Not on the configuration page fetched (only `PHOENIX_PORT`, `PHOENIX_GRPC_PORT`, `PHOENIX_WORKING_DIR`, `PHOENIX_SQL_DATABASE_URL`, `PHOENIX_POSTGRES_*`, and the auth-adjacent password/CSRF/OAuth vars).
- **Braintrust self-hosting / hybrid data plane.** The SDK repo is Apache-2.0, but that says nothing about the backend; no self-hosting statement was fetched. Unresolved. (Angle 4's attempt to fetch the Braintrust CI docs timed out at 60 s twice — the same gap from a second direction.)
- **Opik backing stores** (the page says only "databases, caches etc.") and **Laminar's ClickHouse role** (inferred from a committed `clickhouse-profiles-config.xml`, not from prose).
- **`VLLM_BATCH_INVARIANT` as documented behaviour.** Verified from merged PR/issue text via the GitHub API (PRs 52960, 52998, 53146), **not** from official vLLM documentation. Confirm the flag and per-architecture support in the vLLM docs for the pinned version.
- **LLM-42 performance numbers.** The abs page carries no throughput or overhead figures; only the mechanism is verified.
- **The "Minimum Viable Validation Protocol"** of 2606.19544 — named in the abstract, contents not verified (abs page only). Same for the formal SPB metric definition in 2604.22891.
- **OpenAI's classic "best effort … Determinism is not guaranteed" sentence.** The live docs page fetched does **not** contain it; it says "non-deterministic by default", "(mostly) deterministic outputs", and blames "necessary changes OpenAI makes to model configurations on our end". The "best effort" phrasing appears only in community-forum quotes of the API reference, which was not fetched. **Quote the verified wording, not the folklore.**
- **MT-Bench question count (commonly cited as 80).** Not on the abs page; only "3K expert votes, and 30K conversations".
- **HAX phase groupings** ("initial interaction / during interaction / when the system is wrong / over time") appear on the guidelines page, but the 18 titles came from the library page, which groups by goal instead. The **titles are verified; the title-to-phase mapping is not**.
- **`--allow-playback-repeats`** is not in the pytest-recording README (VCR.py has the kwarg; passing it via `vcr_config` is inference).

**Rejected:**
- **The old `opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/gen-ai/*` URLs as a source of truth** — all 11 pages are now redirect stubs ("no longer maintained in this repository"). Any tooling or doc pinned to them is reading a tombstone.
- **"gen_ai semconv is stable in 2026"** — false. The spec text marks every `gen_ai.*` attribute **Development**; only `error.type`, `server.address`, `server.port` are Stable, and the new repo has no tagged release and a "TODO" schema URL. (A hashnode post asserting "zero stable gen_ai attributes as of 20 July 2026" agrees, but surfaced only as a search snippet — the reliance here is on the spec itself, not the post.)
- **Bit-exact CI gates against hosted provider APIs** — 80 distinct completions from 1,000 identical temperature-0 requests, plus vendor-side config changes signalled only by `system_fingerprint`. Record/replay or statistical gates; nothing else.
- **Lowering temperature to stabilize evals** — Miller shows it can *triple* variance (1/12 → 1/4) and shift the mean (2/3 → 3/4 in the worked case). Use K-resampling.
- **Aggregate score thresholds on a 50-item golden set as a hard CI gate** — MDE ≈ 15.8 pp by the calculation above. It would pass real regressions and fail on noise. Paired per-item flip detection instead.
- **Raw judge-human agreement as the headline quality metric** — 33–41 pp kappa deflation on MT-Bench; Hamel independently warns it "can be misleading when classes are imbalanced".
- **Letting the judge model be the same model as a candidate** — self-preference bias across 20 models, uncorrelated (or negatively correlated) with capability.
- **A single top-level confidence number as the honesty mechanism** — the N=192 study found output-level uncertainty displays lowered users' self-confidence without improving agreement, and relation-level cues *reduced* verification behaviour. Claim-level marks (`orchestrator/claims.py`) are the better lever.
- **A percent-done bar for the pipeline** — fabricated for an unknown-duration task, and against Nielsen's own guidance for that case.
- **`opik.sh --clean` anywhere near CI** — it "destroys all Opik data volumes".
- **NN/g's AI-specific streaming guidance** — no verified URL was reachable after the search budget was exhausted, and an NN/g article that was not fetched will not be cited. The 1993 response-time limits page is fetched and sufficient. Likewise, an arXiv API query for cs.HC papers on streaming + latency + chatbot returned `totalResults = 0`, so there is **no verified source for "latency perception tricks" beyond Nielsen** — treat that sub-topic as an **open gap**, not as covered.

## Angle 6 — Cost, caching, latency & small-model substitution

**Measured baseline for this repo.** Token counts below were taken with `tiktoken` `cl100k_base` against the actual files, not estimated:

| File / group | Tokens |
|---|---|
| `prompts/runtime/*.xml` (all four agents, **byte-identical**) | **4,037** |
| `agents/…/07_judge_logic` system prompt | 646 |
| breaker system prompt | 1,694 |
| creative system prompt | 1,898 |
| logician system prompt | 2,422 |
| `09_synthesizer` system prompt | 3,349 |
| **One query at iteration 1** = `4 × 4,037 + 9,363` | **25,511 static tokens** |

All 25,511 are billed at full input price on every call today. Anthropic's pricing page notes the 4.7+ tokenizer yields "approximately 30% more tokens for the same text", so **treat 25,511 as a floor**. Compare `orchestrator/budget.py` `DEFAULT_TOTAL_TOKENS = 15_000` — the budget is smaller than the prompt it ignores.

| Paper/Source | Link | Verified (what was checked) | Core finding with exact numbers | AETHERIS implementation | E | R | ROI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Anthropic prompt caching doc | [platform.claude.com](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/docs/build-with-claude/prompt-caching) | Fetched full page. Confirmed min-cacheable table, multiplier table, breakpoint rules, prefix order, usage fields | Min cacheable: **512** tok (Opus 5, Fable 5, Mythos 5), **1,024** (Opus 4.8, Sonnet 5, Sonnet 4.6/4.5, Opus 4.1/4, Sonnet 4), **2,048** (Mythos Preview, Opus 4.7, Haiku 3.5), **4,096** (Opus 4.6/4.5, Haiku 4.5); shorter prompts silently uncached, **no error**. 5m write **1.25x**, 1h write **2x**, read **0.1x**. Max **4** breakpoints, **20-block** lookback, breakpoints free. Prefix built in order `tools` → `system` → `messages`; hash is cumulative so any change at or before a breakpoint invalidates it. TTL measured from *start* of the writing/reading request; refreshed free on hit. Pre-warm allowed via `max_tokens: 0`. | OpenAI prompt caching doc | [developers.openai.com](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching) | Fetched after 301 from platform.openai.com. Confirmed thresholds, TTL rules, multipliers, structural rules, field names | **Automatic**, no code change, at **≥1,024 tokens** (older models 1,024–2,048, matching in **128-token increments**). GPT-5.6+ `prompt_cache_options.ttl` accepts only **30m**, refreshed on reuse; earlier models `in_memory` ≈ **5–10 min idle, max 1 hour**, or `24h` extended retention. Reads **0.1x**, writes **1.25x** (GPT-5.6+ only; no write fee before). Explicit rule: put stable instructions/examples/tools/schemas **up front**, request-specific data **last**; push timestamps and debug ids into metadata. `prompt_cache_key` improves routing, keep ≈**15 req/min per key**, "does not make different prompt prefixes match". Fields `cached_tokens` and `cache_write_tokens` under `usage.prompt_tokens_details`. **No latency figures published** | The reorder alone captures this with zero API changes; add `prompt_cache_key` = `f"{role}:{mode}"` in the `client.py` payload for openai/openrouter routes | 0.5d | Low | **Highest** |

| OpenRouter prompt caching doc | [openrouter.ai/docs](https://openrouter.ai/docs/features/prompt-caching) | Fetched on 4th attempt (3 × 60 s timeouts). Confirmed per-provider table, sticky-routing mechanics, `cache_discount`, gotchas | Automatic for most; **Anthropic and Alibaba Qwen require per-message `cache_control`**. Reads: OpenAI 0.25x–0.5x, Grok 0.25x, Moonshot 0.25x, Groq(Kimi) 0.5x, Qwen 0.1x, Anthropic 0.1x, DeepSeek 0.1x, Gemini 0.25x. Sticky routing pins later requests to the same upstream endpoint, expires after **10 min idle**, and identifies a conversation by **hashing the first system message and first non-system message**; a `session_id` (body field or `x-session-id`, ≤256 chars) overrides that and engages stickiness on the **first** request. Inspect `cache_discount`, `cached_tokens`, `cache_write_tokens`. TTLs do **not** translate across providers; Gemini uses only the **last** breakpoint and its `systemInstruction` is immutable once cached | `client.py` must send `session_id` = conversation id. Without it, AETHERIS's four different role system prompts hash to **four separate conversations per query**, fragmenting stickiness and cache warmth | 0.5d | Low | **High** |

Page does **not** state whether it is default-on, and gives **no** perf numbers | `--prefix-caching-hash-algo` = `sha256` (default as of **v0.11**, uses pickle so hashes "may not be reproducible across different Python or vLLM versions"), `sha256_cbor`, `xxhash`, `xxhash_cbor`. **LRU** eviction over an intrusive free queue; finished requests re-queued in reverse (last block least reusable). Only **complete blocks** cached — a 10-token overlap with block size 4 hits **2 blocks / 8 tokens**. ### Implementation ideas, ranked

**1. Reorder the assembled system prompt static→volatile. This is the whole ballgame.**

`agents/prompt_manager.py:353-357` currently emits the per-request block **first**:

```python
parts = [role_block] + runtime_prompts + [agent_prompt]
non_empty_parts = [p for p in parts if p.strip()]
return "\n\n".join(non_empty_parts)
```

`role_block` interpolates `role`, `pipeline_stage`, `objective`, `iteration`, `execution_mode`. Because every provider's cache key is a **cumulative prefix hash** (Anthropic: "changing any block at or before the breakpoint changes the hash"; OpenAI: "put stable instructions… up front and request-specific data last"), putting it at byte 0 means **the cacheable prefix length is zero on every provider**. The 4,037 tokens of `prompts/runtime/*.xml` are byte-identical across Breaker, Logician, Creative and Judge, and are currently re-billed at full price four times per query — plus once more per repair round, because `iteration` bumps.

```python
# agents/prompt_manager.py:353  — ponytail: reorder, not rewrite. One line.
# static → volatile. Shared contracts first (identical for all 4 roles),
# then the role contract, then the only per-request text.
parts = [*runtime_prompts, agent_prompt, role_block]
```

For explicit-breakpoint providers, return segments instead of one string so `client.py` can mark them:

```python
def assemble_agent_prompt_blocks(...) -> list[str]:
    return [
        "\n\n".join(runtime_prompts),  # 4,037 tok — breakpoint 1, shared by all roles
        agent_prompt,                  #  646–3,349 tok — breakpoint 2, per role
        role_block,                    #     ~60 tok — never cached
    ]
```

Two breakpoints, well inside Anthropic's limit of 4. Both blocks clear every published minimum (Anthropic 512–4,096, OpenAI 1,024, Gemini 1,024–4,096, Groq 128–1,024).

Economics per query on Sonnet 5 ($2 in / $2.50 5m-write / $0.20 read):

| Case | Arithmetic | System-prompt cost/query | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| **today** | 25,511 tok × $2/MTok | **$0.0510** | — |
| **warm** (query within the last 5 min) | 25,511 × $0.20/MTok | **$0.0051** | **−90%** |
| **cold** | 1 shared write + 3 shared reads + 4 role writes = $0.0101 + $0.0024 + $0.0234 | **$0.0359** | **−30%** |

Ordering inside the query already helps: the Breaker runs first and returns before the Logician∥Creative fan-out at `orchestrator/decisions.py:508`, so its write warms the shared block for the other three — exactly what Anthropic's "concurrent requests can't share a cache entry until the first response begins" demands. That ordering is already correct today; **the reorder is what makes it pay.**

*Caveat:* the role-specific block is read only once per query, so it only earns back its 1.25x write **across** queries. Below ~1 query per 5 min it is a **25% loss** — gate it, or use the 1 h TTL (2x write, break-even at two reads).

**2. Harvest cached-token telemetry, or you are flying blind and mispricing by 10x.**

`api_gateway/client.py:122` is `observer.track_usage(actual_model, usage.get("prompt_tokens", 0), usage.get("completion_tokens", 0))`. It discards every cache field. Because `telemetry/observer.py` prices with `cost = input×in_rate + output×out_rate` and has no third bucket, cached reads billed at 0.1x are counted at 1.0x — **turning on caching would make reported cost go up.** Provider field names differ and all four variants must be read:

| Provider | Fields |
|---|---|
| OpenAI, Groq, OpenRouter | `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, `…cache_write_tokens` |
| DeepSeek | `usage.prompt_cache_hit_tokens`, `usage.prompt_cache_miss_tokens` |
| Anthropic native | `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` |

Langfuse's warning is the trap: buckets must be mutually exclusive or inferred cost "overstates what your provider actually charged", and OTel's registry says cache_read/cache_write **are included in** `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens`. Emit `gen_ai.usage.cache_read.input_tokens` and `gen_ai.usage.cache_write.input_tokens` by those exact names; cached-token ratio is **not** expressible through `gen_ai.token.type`, whose enum is only `input`/`output`.

**3. Send `session_id` on every OpenRouter call.** OpenRouter identifies a conversation by hashing the first system message and the first non-system message. AETHERIS sends four different role system prompts per query, so it presents as **four separate conversations**, and sticky routing — which is what keeps the upstream cache warm, and expires after 10 minutes idle — never consolidates. A `session_id` body field (or `x-session-id`, ≤256 chars) overrides the hash and engages stickiness on the first request rather than waiting for an observed hit. One line in the `client.py` payload.

**4. Skip the Judge when Logician and Creative agree.** `orchestrator/decisions.py:_execute_parallel` already produces two heterogeneous views; ICLR 2024 ([2310.03094](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.03094)) shows weak-model answer consistency across a mixture of thought representations reaches strong-model accuracy at **40% of its cost**. `orchestrator/consensus.py` (282 lines) already exists. This removes one of 3–5 calls on the agreeing fraction — **the single largest reduction available in call *count* rather than call *price***. Calibrate the agreement threshold against the CRC feasibility test first: if the Judge's base error μ exceeds your target α, [2606.29054](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29054) proves you must defer on at least `(μ−α)/(1−α)` of queries regardless of implementation.

**5. Fix the Groq model choice.** `api_gateway/strategy.py` routes `groq/llama-3.3-70b-versatile` and `groq/llama-3.1-8b-instant`. Groq's caching doc lists exactly three eligible models — `openai/gpt-oss-20b`, `openai/gpt-oss-120b`, `openai/gpt-oss-safeguard-20b` — so today AETHERIS's Groq traffic gets **0%** of the available 50% cached-input discount. Switching unlocks it on the 5,731–7,386-token static prefix, with a 2-hour TTL that comfortably spans a query. *(Angle-level cross-check: the model-landscape angle independently found Groq's production list contains **no Llama models at all**, so these two IDs are also on track to 404.)*

**6. Regenerate `pricing.json` from the OpenRouter models API.** The file's own `_meta` says "SEED ESTIMATES ONLY — VERIFY against live provider price sheets", and it still lists `claude-3.5-sonnet` and prices Gemini/Groq/GitHub at 0.0. `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models` returns `input_cache_read`, `input_cache_write` and `input_cache_write_1h` per model as exact decimal strings. A ~30-line refresh script gives real cached rates and kills a whole class of wrong cost telemetry.

**7. Make `orchestrator/budget.py` see the system prompt.** `DEFAULT_TOTAL_TOKENS = 15_000` while the static contracts alone are ~25,511 tokens per query. The budget counts history and output only, so pressure states (`normal`/`tight`/`critical` at 0.80/0.90) fire against the wrong denominator, and `orchestrator/prediction.py`'s `expected_cost_usd` default of $0.01 is uncalibrated against reality. Count the assembled prompt, and **weight cached tokens at 0.1x so the budget rewards cache hits instead of punishing them.**

**8. Stream the LLM calls.** `client.py` never sets `stream: true`, so each of 3–5 calls blocks for its full response. `orchestrator/streaming.py` already defines `DRAFT_ANSWER` and stage events over SSE, so the plumbing to the browser exists — only the upstream leg is non-streaming. Streaming the Judge alone moves perceived TTFT from "whole pipeline" to "Judge's first token". *(Tension to resolve: the model-landscape angle notes streaming is mutually exclusive with the Batch API's 50% discount — pick per stage, not globally.)*

**9. Set reasoning budgets per stage, not globally.** [2508.12140](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12140) gives measured regimes: **0–256** tokens high-efficiency, **256–512** the cost-performance optimum, **>512** "justified only for critical diagnostic tasks". `prompts/system/10_reasoning_budget.xml` (544 tok) is the natural home: Gate and Judge in 256–512, only the Logician above it. On Anthropic this maps onto `output_config.effort` (default **high**) directly.

**10. Do not compress the XML contracts.** Compression's measured end-to-end ceiling is **18%** (ECIR 2026) and it costs a compressor forward pass per call; a cache read is 90% off with zero quality risk. Worse, CDCT finds constraint-compliance violations peak at **medium** compression (c=0.5) with constraint effects **2.9x** semantic effects, and CAPC measured query-aware compression coming out **+40.1% more expensive than sending the full prompt** on τ-bench because the per-query prefix invalidates the cache every call. AETHERIS's contracts are pure instruction density — **the worst possible compression target.** Trim them by hand instead: [2608.17188](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17188)'s 50:50-fill condition beat 100% fill by **+0.077** (d=0.49), so shortening is plausibly a quality win as well as a cost one — but measure it.

**11. Defer any response cache.** Plan for ~**20%** real hit rate (Portkey's measured production figure at 99% accuracy), not the 53–68% in papers. If built, all five of these are load-bearing:
- per-entry learned thresholds with an explicit error budget — vCache: **12.5x** hit rate, **26x** lower error than a static cutoff;
- a dependency key covering the 26 contract-file hashes + retrieved-doc versions + model id + decoding params — FinCacheServe: **53.27%** of calls skipped, **zero** observed stale outputs;
- per-tenant namespacing;
- write-gating to Judge-accepted answers only — CacheAttack hijacks embedding-keyed caches at **86%**;
- per-tier staleness budgets — FreshCache ε = 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.35.

**12. Small models: substitute at the Gate, never at the Judge.** The Breaker Gate is a binary knowledge-absence classifier behind a 5,731-token prompt — [2607.27172](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27172) hit 80% traffic coverage at **~30% of teacher cost** with a distilled LoRA SLM, and [2608.07169](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07169) got **+27.2 pp** on AppWorld with 4B–8B students. The Judge is the opposite case: a 0.5B self-judge put **60% of its picks on one index** ([2606.06454](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06454)), and [2606.19544](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19544) shows judge κ deflating **33–41 pp** while test-retest stays **>0.95** — so any swap must be validated on **κ and position bias, not agreement**.

**13. Self-hosting is a later, separate decision.** If a local judge/detector lands: vLLM prefix caching is block-granular with LRU eviction, `--prefix-caching-hash-algo` defaulting to `sha256` as of v0.11, and a per-request **`cache_salt`** that the docs explicitly frame as blocking "timing-based attacks where an adversary could infer cached content by observing latency differences" — set it per tenant on day one. Budget ~4x KV headroom for ~5.8 pp accuracy (DistillCache: 94.2% of full-cache at a 25% budget, 2.1x throughput) or 5.8x near-lossless (AATC). Expect **~2.0x average** from speculative decoding, not the 5.3x headline — and note [2607.21804](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21804)'s adversarial acceptance collapse, **+62.3% mean sample time**, a live latency-DoS risk given AETHERIS accepts arbitrary user queries.

---

### Cheap wins (≤1 day each)

1. **The reorder** — `agents/prompt_manager.py:353-357`, one line, plus updating whichever tests assert the assembled string. Unlocks OpenAI, Gemini implicit, DeepSeek and Groq-gpt-oss caching with **no API changes at all**, because all four are automatic. Verify by asserting `cached_tokens > 0` on the second call.
2. **Harvest cache fields** — extend `observer.track_usage` with a `cached_input_tokens` bucket and read all four provider field-name variants at `client.py:122`. **Without this you cannot tell whether win #1 worked.**
3. **`session_id` on OpenRouter calls** — one payload field; consolidates four fragmented sticky-routing conversations into one.
4. **Groq model swap** — `strategy.py` `FREE_MODELS`/`HYBRID_MODELS`: `groq/llama-*` → `groq/openai/gpt-oss-120b`. Zero-to-50% on cached input.
5. **Refresh `pricing.json` + `observer.MODEL_PRICING`** from the OpenRouter models endpoint, adding `cache_read` / `cache_write_5m` / `cache_write_1h`. Both tables are currently 2024-vintage.
6. **Move the "Instruction Reinforcement" reminder** (`client.py:56-66`, ~120 static tokens × 2 variants) out of the volatile tail and into `prompts/runtime/02_response_contract.xml`, where it becomes cacheable. Small money, but it also stops static text sitting **after** OpenAI's implicit breakpoint. *(The model-landscape angle argues for deleting this block outright now that structured outputs are GA — deletion dominates relocation.)*
7. **Pass `latency_s` to `track_usage`** — the parameter exists and defaults to 0.0, and neither call site ever supplies it, so `total_latency_s` is permanently zero. Also delete the hardcoded fake `sparkline_history` seed.
8. **Emit the two ratios** that actually govern this workload: `cache_read.input_tokens / input_tokens`, and cost per Judge-accepted answer (`total_cost / judge_accepted_count` — OTel defines **no** standard name for it, confirmed by fetching both the metrics doc and the attribute registry).
9. **Count the system prompt in `budget.py`** so the 15,000-token budget stops ignoring the 25,511-token dominant term.

---

### Unverified / rejected (Angle 6)

All table rows above were fetched during the research pass; a subset of the compression, semantic-caching, cascade and observability rows was fetched by a delegated sub-pass and its verification notes are reproduced as stated there.

**Conflicts found between sources — first-party wins:**
- OpenRouter's minimum-prompt-size table says "Claude Opus 4.5–4.8 and Haiku 4.5: 4096", but Anthropic's own doc puts **Opus 4.8 at 1,024** and only 4.6/4.5 at 4,096. OpenRouter's table is stale.
- OpenRouter lists Gemini cache **reads at 0.25x**; Google's own pricing page shows cached input at exactly **10% of standard** on every caching-capable model. Use Google's.
- OpenRouter says OpenAI reads are "0.25x or 0.5x by model"; OpenAI's own doc says **0.1x**. Routing through OpenRouter may genuinely capture less of the discount — worth **measuring via `cache_discount`, not assuming**.

**Unverified:**
- **SGLang / RadixAttention status.** `docs.sglang.ai/advanced_features/radix_cache.html` 301s to `docs.sglang.io`, which then timed out at 60 s. No SGLang claim is made anywhere in this angle.
- **TensorRT-LLM, llama.cpp, Ollama, LMCache, Mooncake, NVIDIA Dynamo current status.** Not fetched. The self-hosting row rests on vLLM's own design doc only.
- **Whether vLLM prefix caching is on by default, and any vLLM throughput number.** The design doc documents flags, eviction and limitations but states neither. Its only perf claim is the qualitative "almost a free lunch".
- **EAGLE-3 / Medusa specifics.** Not fetched; the speculative-decoding rows are 2026 arXiv entries retrieved via the arXiv API instead.
- **Mistral and NVIDIA NIM caching.** Not fetched. AETHERIS routes to both (`client.py`); **assume no caching until confirmed.**
- **DeepSeek minimum cacheable chunk size.** The KV-cache doc says units form "at fixed token intervals" without naming a number.
- **Any published latency figure for prompt caching from OpenAI or Anthropic.** Neither page gives one. The only quantified TTFT number in this angle is [2601.06007](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06007)'s **13–31%**.
- **LLMLingua / LLMLingua-2 numeric quality deltas.** Both abstracts say only "little performance loss" / "significant performance gains over strong baselines".
- **A "GPTCache 3.3% hit rate" figure** seen in a search snippet but absent from the fetched abstract page, which attributes 37.9% to real benchmarks. Not quotable.
- **Total providers audited in [2502.07776](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07776).** Only "seven providers with global cache sharing, including OpenAI" is confirmed.
- **OpenAI prompt-caching latency percentages, and RouteLLM per-benchmark numbers.** Neither appears on the fetched pages. *(See also the RouteLLM correction in the memory-corrections section.)*

**Rejected:**
- **All SEO/derivative prompt-caching pages** (`docs.apiyi.com`, `startdebugging.net`, `futureagi.com`, `edenai.co`, `jangwook.net`, `tech-insider.org`, `dev.to`, `orq.ai`, `costgoat.com`) — every one restates Anthropic/OpenAI/Google multipliers second-hand. First-party docs fetched instead. The dev.to claim that Anthropic "silently changed the default TTL from 1 hour to 5 minutes on March 6, 2026" is **not supported** by Anthropic's own page, which documents 5 minutes as the default with 1 hour as opt-in and carries no changelog.
- **`developers.googleblog.com` "same 75% token discount"** — contradicted by Google's current pricing page, which shows cached input at 10% of standard (a 90% discount) across the board. Stale 2.5-era figure.
- **`discuss.ai.google.dev` thread on implicit cache regressions** — user report, no methodology; useful as a risk signal only (implicit caching can silently stop applying), not as a number.
- **`tianpan.co`, `metacto.com`, `ml4devs.com`, `redis.io` semantic-cache hit-rate claims** — either re-citations of arXiv:2411.05276 or figures with no stated methodology or workload.
- **`pointfive.co` "Top 10 Prompt Compression Solutions (2026)"** and **`sitepoint.com` "up to 63%"** — vendor listicle and tutorial, unsourced savings claims. The same conclusions are properly verified via [2607.15516](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15516) and [2601.06007](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06007).
- **`microsoft.com` LongLLMLingua page (17.1%)** and **`iclr.cc` virtual page (94.2%)** — both conflict by one digit with arXiv v2's 21.4% and 94.0%. Stale mirrors.
- **`hexdocs.pm` OpenTelemetry.SemConv v1.27.0** and third-party OTel restatements (`harness.io`, `openobserve.ai`, Microsoft's fork) — pinned to 2024 snapshots that predate the `gen_ai.usage.cache_read/cache_write` attributes. Canonical repo fetched instead.
- **PD-disaggregation and 3D-stacking work** (arXiv:2608.03741, 2607.01617, 2607.18002, 2607.00466, 2607.25498) — verified and real, but AETHERIS is an API client. Recorded **only to justify not building any of it**.
- **Datacenter-scale carbon papers** (arXiv:2608.12915, 2605.11603, 2605.05615, 2601.08113) — no per-query Wh, gCO2e or water figures in their abstracts, and the wrong altitude for a client-side orchestrator. The energy angle is covered by [2605.13496](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13496) and [2608.19535](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19535) at one line each.

**Verdict for Angle 6:** *the cheapest 90% cost cut in this repo is a one-line list reorder — and it is currently unmeasurable, because `client.py:122` throws away every field that would prove it worked. Ship win #2 before win #1.*

---

## Angle 7 — KV cache, speculative decoding & the serving stack

**Scope note.** This angle asks a different question from Angle 6. Angle 6 asked *what can an httpx API client do about cost*; this one asks *what happens inside an inference engine, and which of it is reachable from AETHERIS today*. The answer to the second question is mostly **"none of it, until a model is self-hosted"** — which is itself the finding. Section E is the exception: the tricks there work over plain completion APIs.

**Deliberate overlap with Angle 6, kept per "don't lose any papers or ideas."** vLLM prefix caching, OasisKV (2608.08097), spec-decode acceptance collapse and PD disaggregation appear in both angles. Angle 6 read them as *cost* evidence; here they are read as *serving mechanics*, and the verified detail differs — e.g. Angle 6 could not confirm whether vLLM prefix caching is default-on; this angle resolves it from the `CacheConfig` API page (and finds the two vLLM doc pages contradict each other).

**Date convention.** Dates are arXiv `published` timestamps as shown on the fetched page. Where an arXiv ID's `YYMM` prefix disagrees with the page's stated submission date, it is flagged in UNVERIFIED rather than silently reconciled. Effort/risk are not per-row here (the source pass did not estimate them); they are folded into the ranked ideas below.

### A. KV cache: prefix reuse, eviction/compression, cross-request, offload

| Source | Link | Verified (what was checked) | Core finding with exact numbers | Relevance to AETHERIS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SGLang / RadixAttention (Zheng et al.) | [arxiv.org/abs/2312.07104](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.07104) | Fetched abs page; title "SGLang: Efficient Execution of Structured Language Model Programs"; abstract contains "up to 6.4x higher throughput"; v2 6 Jun 2024 | RadixAttention = radix tree over KV blocks for cross-request prefix reuse + compressed FSM decoding; **up to 6.4x higher throughput** vs SOTA on agent control, JSON decoding, RAG, multi-turn | Origin of the prefix-tree idea that makes 3–5 calls sharing one system prompt cheap. Only matters if you self-host; API-side you get it via provider prompt caching (row E1) |
| PEEK (queue-informed radix scheduling) | [arxiv.org/abs/2607.02525](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02525) | Fetched abs page; title "PEEK: Predictive Queue-Informed KV Cache Management for LLM Serving"; abstract contains the per-engine gains below; 26 pp / 21 figs | Radix tree over the **pending queue** (not just the cache), admits cluster "pioneers" first, protects ancestor blocks from eviction. vs best stock SGLang/vLLM baseline: **cache hit 3.0x / 2.6x, TTFT 7.9x / 7.1x, end-to-end 6.7x / 5.5x, throughput 3.6x / 4.5x**; parity "within noise" when no prefix structure exists | Directly models AETHERIS's pattern: N concurrent queries whose stage-1 prompts share a prefix. **Order-of-admission alone is worth multiples — no model change** |
| SGLANG-LSM (LSM-tree KV store) | [arxiv.org/abs/2511.16138](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16138) | Fetched abs page; title "On 10x Better Scalability: KV Stores Scale Up KV Cache"; abstract contains "by up to 143%" and "by up to 24%"; v2 25 Nov 2025 | File-per-object disk KV layouts hit metadata/locality walls; LSM-tree storage engine gives **cache hits +143%, TTFT −24%** on large dynamic workloads | Only relevant at disk-tier scale. Skip unless a judge cache outgrows RAM |
| RelayCaching (multi-agent decode-KV reuse) | [arxiv.org/abs/2603.13289](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13289) | Fetched abs page; title matches; abstract contains "over 80% KV cache reuse", "reduces TTFT by up to 4.7x", "negligible accuracy degradation"; 28 Feb 2026 | Training-free: reuse agent N's **decode-phase** KV as agent N+1's prefill KV. Divergence from the prefix change is "sparse and localized" to few layers/positions; recompute only those. **>80% reuse, TTFT up to 4.7x lower** | **The single most on-point paper for AETHERIS's topology:** stage K's output is stage K+1's input, re-prefilled from scratch today. Requires engine-level access (self-hosted only) |
| CacheScout (agent-aware KV runtime) | [arxiv.org/abs/2608.14624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14624) | Fetched abs page; title "Learning Agent Execution for KV-Cache Management in Agentic Serving"; abstract contains all six figures below; built on vLLM | Recency-LRU evicts agent contexts before their next call. Learns agent transitions **online, no workflow graph, no offline training**: **hit rate +10–18 pp, mean TTFT −18–45%, mean per-turn latency −29–38%, peak throughput +57%**; larger models **TTFT −54%, throughput +37%** | Same failure mode you would hit self-hosting a judge alongside main-path calls: the judge prompt is evicted between invocations. **Prefetch/pin beats LRU** |
| ReCache (tool-augmented agents) | [arxiv.org/abs/2608.19662](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19662) | Fetched abs page; title matches; abstract contains "82.3% versus 82.4% Inv-F1", "3.655x time-to-first-token speedup", "92.43%", "1.423x"; 20 Aug 2026 | Tool/skill schemas recur in varying **order**, defeating strict-prefix caching. Resource-wise attention with resource-local positions makes KV order-independent: **Inv-F1 82.3% vs 82.4% dense, 3.655x TTFT, KV memory −92.43%, attention 1.423x** | If AETHERIS sends tool/function schemas, ordering them non-deterministically **silently destroys prefix hits**. Cheap fix: freeze tool array order (see E1) |
| IntentKV (cross-turn agent pruning) | [arxiv.org/abs/2606.09916](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09916) | Verified via `export.arxiv.org` API (official arXiv endpoint); title "IntentKV: Cross-Turn Intent-Aware KV Cache Pruning for Agent Inference"; abstract numbers as quoted; 6 Jun 2026. Abs HTML page **not** separately fetched | At 8k KV budget mean peak request tokens **−23.9% (Qwen3-8B) / −30.7% (Qwen2.5-14B)**; worst-case peak **92.3k → 20.5k (−77.8%)**, raw KV reads **411M → 31M (−92.6%)** | Sizing data for a small self-hosted model with agent-shaped context |
| "Models Take Notes at Prefill" | [arxiv.org/abs/2606.17107](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17107) | Fetched abs page; title "Models Take Notes at Prefill: KV Cache Can Be Editable and Composable"; abstract contains "98.5% hit-rate", "53-398x", "14.9x", "logit cosine 0.90-0.999"; **single author**, 14 Jun 2026 | A field's own KV is **<1% of the decision** (causal, 4 model families) — so editing that field alone fails without CoT, succeeds with it (**1.00 at 8B, ~1% compute**). Skills are position-portable via RoPE shift, TTFT **O(L) not O(L²)**. Online vLLM: **98.5% hit-rate, p90 TTFT cut 53–398x**; agent **14.9x lower latency**. Append-only, so it layers on production prefix caching | Strongest claimed numbers in the set and explicitly composable with existing prefix caching. **Single-author, unreplicated — treat as a research direction, not a dependency** |
| H2O | [arxiv.org/abs/2306.14048](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.14048) | Fetched abs page; title "H₂O: Heavy-Hitter Oracle…"; abstract contains "20% heavy hitters", "up to 29x, 29x, and 3x", "reduce the latency by up to 1.9x"; v3 18 Dec 2023 | Attention mass concentrates on few tokens; keep recent + heavy hitters. **20% budget**; throughput **up to 29x / 29x / 3x** vs DeepSpeed ZeRO-Inference / HF Accelerate / FlexGen (OPT-6.7B/30B); **latency 1.9x lower** at equal batch | Baseline everything else is measured against. **Note the 29x baselines are weak offloading systems, not vLLM** |
| SnapKV | [arxiv.org/abs/2404.14469](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14469) | Fetched abs page; title "SnapKV: LLM Knows What You are Looking for Before Generation"; abstract contains "3.6x increase in generation speed and an 8.2x enhancement in memory efficiency", "16K tokens", "380K context tokens on a single A100-80GB GPU"; v2 17 Jun 2024 | Per-head clustered selection from an end-of-prompt observation window, **fine-tuning-free**. At 16K input: **3.6x generation speed, 8.2x memory efficiency**; **380K ctx on one A100-80GB**; negligible NIAH drop over 16 datasets. Abstract states **no explicit retained-fraction %** | Best quality/effort ratio of the classical methods, and the 2026 benchmark (row below) picks it for throughput |
| PyramidKV | [arxiv.org/abs/2406.02069](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02069) | Fetched abs page; title matches; abstract contains "retaining only 12% of the KV cache", "only 0.7% of the KV cache", "20.5 absolute accuracy improvement", "128 KV cache entries … 100.0 Acc"; v4 15 May 2025 | Layer-varying budget (big low, small high) from attention funneling. **Matches full cache at 12% of KV**; at **0.7%** still beats peers; **+20.5 absolute** on TREC; **128 entries → 100.0 Acc** NIAH on Llama-3-70B | The cleanest "compression ratio + quality delta" pair in the literature |
| StreamingLLM | [arxiv.org/abs/2309.17453](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.17453) | Fetched abs page; title "Efficient Streaming Language Models with Attention Sinks"; abstract contains "up to 4 million tokens and more", "up to 22.2x speedup"; ICLR 2024, v4 7 Apr 2024 | Keeping the first few tokens (attention sinks) restores window attention's quality; **4M+ tokens**, **22.2x** vs sliding-window recompute, no fine-tuning | **Wrong tool for AETHERIS** — solves unbounded streams, not repeated short prompts |
| KVQuant | [arxiv.org/abs/2401.18079](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.18079) | Fetched abs page; title "KVQuant: Towards 10 Million Context Length…"; abstract contains "< 0.1 perplexity degradation with 3-bit quantization", "1 million on a single A100-80GB", "up to ~1.7x speedups"; NeurIPS 2024, v6 28 May 2025 | Per-channel + pre-RoPE key quant, sensitivity-weighted non-uniform datatypes, dense-and-sparse outlier split: **<0.1 ppl degradation at 3-bit**; **1M ctx on one A100-80GB / 10M on 8 GPUs**; **~1.7x** kernel speedup vs fp16 matvec | **3-bit KV is the cheapest way to fit a judge's KV on a small GPU.** Consumer-GPU judge: this is the knob to reach for before any compression scheme |
| KV-cache optimization benchmark (Agrawal & Mayer) | [arxiv.org/abs/2607.05399](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05399) | Fetched abs page; title "Benchmarking KV-Cache Optimizations across Task Quality and System Performance for Long-Context Serving"; abstract contains "the compression ratio alone is a poor predictor of end-to-end performance", "KIVI4 provides the most stable quality", "SnapKV delivers the strongest long-context throughput" | Compares KIVI, TurboQuant, SnapKV, CaM on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct + Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3 across LongBench-style tasks. **Compression ratio does not predict end-to-end performance.** KIVI4 = most stable quality; SnapKV = best long-ctx throughput; CaM = big wins on some QA but "substantial workload sensitivity". **No numeric values on the abs page** | The reason not to trust any single compression paper's headline. **If you compress the judge's KV, benchmark on *your* workload** |
| Learning to Evict / KVP (Apple) | [arxiv.org/abs/2602.10238](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10238) | Fetched abs page; title "Learning to Evict from Key-Value Cache"; abstract contains "learning to rank tokens by their predicted usefulness", "up to 128K tokens"; ICML 2026; v2 26 Jun 2026; code `github.com/apple/ml-learning-to-evict` | Recency/attention heuristics are indirect proxies. RL: **lightweight per-head agents** trained on generation traces from K/V vectors only, one reward valid **across all cache budgets**, "no modifications to the underlying LLM or additional inference". Zero-shot transfer to BoolQ / LongBench retrieval / GovReport | The 2026 successor direction to H2O/SnapKV. **No numbers on the abs page** — see UNVERIFIED |
| CacheTune (non-prefix reuse + tiering) | [arxiv.org/abs/2605.24022](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24022) | Fetched abs page; title "Adaptive KV Cache Reuse for Fast Long-Context LLM Serving"; abstract contains "3.72x-4.86x", "3.93x-6.21x", "2.34x-2.36x"; 20 May 2026 | Frequency-domain offline ranking of KV pairs critical to cross-attention recovery, then recompute only those online. **TTFT 3.72–4.86x, throughput 3.93–6.21x**; with SSD/HDD-offloaded cache **TTFT 2.34–2.36x**. Adaptive recompute-ratio knob trades GPU compute against slow fetch | **The tuning knob matters more than the algorithm:** an adjustable recompute ratio is what makes offload survive real storage |
| Tutti (GPU-centric NVMe KV store) | [arxiv.org/abs/2605.03375](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03375) | Fetched abs page; title "Tutti: Making SSD-Backed KV Cache Practical for Long-Context LLM Serving"; abstract contains "reduces TTFT by 78.3% under strict SLO constraints", "improves the achievable request rate by 2x", "The serving cost is reduced by 27%"; 5 May 2026; built on vLLM | Scattered GPU layouts cause many small random I/Os; even GDS needs the CPU to launch each I/O. GPU-native object store + "GPU io_uring", CPU only loads I/O kernels once per layer. vs **GDS-enabled SSD-backed LMCache: TTFT −78.3%, request rate 2x, cost −27%**; near DRAM-backed LMCache parity | Establishes that NVMe KV tiers are **I/O-path-bound, not bandwidth-bound**. Relevant only if the judge cache goes to disk |
| SwiftCache (cross-model memory donation) | [arxiv.org/abs/2606.16135](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16135) | Fetched abs page; title "SwiftCache: Efficient LLM Serving for Multi-turn Conversations with Heterogeneous KV Cache Sharing"; abstract contains "P99 time-to-first-token (TTFT) by up to 69%", "3.98x"; 15 Jun 2026 | Co-located heterogeneous models donate idle GPU memory over **NVLink** to hold each other's prefix cache; only the active layer's KV stays local. vs vLLM and SGLang: **P99 TTFT −69%, max context 3.98x**, minimal interference | The exact scenario of "judge model co-located with something else on one GPU". **No accuracy metric reported** |
| KVRouting (eviction × routing, ICLR 2026) | [arxiv.org/abs/2601.18999](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18999) | Fetched abs page; title "Randomization Boosts KV Caching, Learning Balances Query Load: A Joint Perspective"; abstract contains "up to 6.92x in cache hit rate, 11.96x reduction in latency", "14.06x reduction in time-to-first-token (TTFT), and 77.4% increase in throughput"; 26 Jan 2026 | First joint model of the load-balancing vs per-worker-hit-rate conflict. Randomized eviction with competitive guarantees + learned router: **hit rate 6.92x, latency 11.96x, TTFT 14.06x, throughput +77.4%** over prior SOTA, on 4 benchmarks × 3 prefix-sharing settings | Confirms the tension you would face with >1 judge replica: **round-robin destroys cache hits. Route by prefix, not evenly** |
| Mooncake (Kimi) | [arxiv.org/abs/2407.00079](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00079) | Fetched abs page; title "Mooncake: A KVCache-centric Disaggregated Architecture for LLM Serving"; abstract contains "up to a 525% increase in throughput in certain simulated scenarios while adhering to SLOs", "75% more requests"; v4 3 Sep 2025 | Prefill/decode clusters split; distributed KVCache from idle CPU/DRAM/SSD in the GPU cluster; KVCache-centric scheduler + **predictive early rejection** under overload. **Up to 525% throughput in certain simulated scenarios**; Kimi handles **75% more requests** | **The 525% is simulated — cite carefully.** The transferable idea is early rejection when overloaded, which AETHERIS's FastAPI layer can do cheaply |
| DistServe (OSDI '24) | [arxiv.org/abs/2401.09670](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09670) | Fetched abs page; title "DistServe: Disaggregating Prefill and Decoding for Goodput-optimized Large Language Model Serving"; abstract contains "7.4x more requests or 12.6x tighter SLO", "> 90% of requests"; v3 6 Jun 2024 | Colocating prefill and decode couples TTFT and TPOT into one plan. Disaggregating + per-phase parallelism: **7.4x more requests or 12.6x tighter SLO** vs SOTA, within latency bounds for **>90%** of requests | Canonical P/D result. **Multi-GPU only — irrelevant to a single-GPU judge** |
| LMCache (docs) | [docs.lmcache.ai](https://docs.lmcache.ai/) · [benchmarking](https://docs.lmcache.ai/getting_started/benchmarking.html) | Fetched both. Landing page: non-prefix reuse via CacheBlend, tiers "CPU RAM, local disk (SSD), Redis/Valkey, Mooncake, InfiniStore, S3-compatible object storage, NIXL, and GDS"; MP mode since an April 2026 release, newest update entry 2026/06; **no release number stated**. Benchmarking page: table + prose | Reuses KV blocks **at any position** (not just prefix) via CacheBlend, recomputing selectively. Measured on the docs' own `long-doc-qa`, Qwen3-8B, 4 in-flight, 10 GB KV volume, 10,000-token docs: **mean TTFT 757 ms → 185 ms (−75%)**, benchmark duration **23.47 s → 13.79 s (−41%)**, 46 successful requests both runs. Gain attributed to **L1 CPU-RAM** offload, not a remote tier | The cleanest verified CPU-offload number available, on exactly the 8B class you would use for a judge. **Caveat: a warmup round primes the cache, so this is a best-case hit-rate scenario** |

### B. Speculative decoding, 2026 state
